  \texttt{record\ Rectangle\ \{\ width:\ f64,\ height:\ f64\ \}}.
\item
  Write a generic function
  \texttt{print\_area{[}T\ where\ T:\ Area{]}(shape:\ T)} that calls
  \texttt{area} and prints the result.
\end{enumerate}

\section{Chapter 7: Traits and
Generics}\label{chapter-7-traits-and-generics}

IRIS features a robust type system that supports traits and generics,
allowing for clean code reuse, static polymorphism, and generic
programming.

\subsection{7.1 Trait Declarations}\label{trait-declarations-1}

A \textbf{trait} defines a contract or interface of method signatures
that types must satisfy:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{trait}\NormalTok{ Printable \{}
    \KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ to\_string(self: Self) }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{str}
\NormalTok{\}}

\KeywordTok{trait}\NormalTok{ Comparable \{}
    \KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ compare(self: Self, other: Self) }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

The keyword \texttt{Self} (capitalized) inside a trait definition
represents the type that will implement the trait.

\subsection{7.2 Implementing Traits}\label{implementing-traits-1}

Use the \texttt{impl} keyword to implement a trait for a concrete record
type:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{record}\NormalTok{ Point \{}
\NormalTok{    x: }\DataTypeTok{f64}\NormalTok{,}
\NormalTok{    y: }\DataTypeTok{f64}\NormalTok{,}
\NormalTok{\}}

\KeywordTok{impl}\NormalTok{ Printable }\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ Point \{}
    \KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ to\_string(self: Point) }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{str}\NormalTok{ \{}
        \FunctionTok{format}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"(\{\}, \{\})"}\NormalTok{, self}\OperatorTok{.}\NormalTok{x, self}\OperatorTok{.}\NormalTok{y)}
\NormalTok{    \}}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Once a trait is implemented, its methods can be called on instances of
that type:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ main() }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ p }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ Point \{ x: }\FloatTok{3.5}\NormalTok{, y: }\OperatorTok{{-}}\FloatTok{2.0}\NormalTok{ \};}
    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(p}\OperatorTok{.}\NormalTok{to\_string());}
    \DecValTok{0}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsection{7.3 Generic Functions}\label{generic-functions-1}

Generic functions declare type parameters inside square brackets
\texttt{{[}T{]}}:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ identity[T](x: T) }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}}\NormalTok{ T \{}
\NormalTok{    x}
\NormalTok{\}}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ my\_max[T](a: T, b: T) }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}}\NormalTok{ T \{}
    \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ a }\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}=}\NormalTok{ b \{ a \} }\ControlFlowTok{else}\NormalTok{ \{ b \}}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Generics in IRIS are \textbf{monomorphized} at compile time, generating
efficient concrete implementations for each type used.

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{7.4 Trait Constraints
(\texttt{where})}{7.4 Trait Constraints (where)}}\label{trait-constraints-where-1}

You can constrain generic parameters using the \texttt{where} keyword,
enforcing that types must implement specific traits:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ print\_item[T }\KeywordTok{where}\NormalTok{ T: Printable](x: T) }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(x}\OperatorTok{.}\NormalTok{to\_string());}
    \DecValTok{0}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsection{Try It Yourself}\label{try-it-yourself-6}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Define a trait \texttt{Area} with a method
  \texttt{area(self:\ Self)\ -\textgreater{}\ f64}.
\item
  Implement \texttt{Area} for
  \texttt{record\ Circle\ \{\ radius:\ f64\ \}} and
  \texttt{record\ Rectangle\ \{\ width:\ f64,\ height:\ f64\ \}}.
\item
  Write a generic function
  \texttt{print\_area{[}T\ where\ T:\ Area{]}(shape:\ T)} that calls
  \texttt{area} and prints the result.
\end{enumerate}

\section{Chapter 8: Closures and Higher-Order
Functions}\label{chapter-8-closures-and-higher-order-functions}

\subsection{8.1 Closure Syntax}\label{closure-syntax}

A closure is an anonymous function that can capture values from its
surrounding scope. The syntax is
\texttt{\textbar{}param:\ Type\textbar{}\ expr}:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ main() }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ double }\OperatorTok{=} \OperatorTok{|}\NormalTok{x: }\DataTypeTok{i64}\OperatorTok{|}\NormalTok{ x }\OperatorTok{*} \DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{;}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ add\_ten }\OperatorTok{=} \OperatorTok{|}\NormalTok{x: }\DataTypeTok{i64}\OperatorTok{|}\NormalTok{ x }\OperatorTok{+} \DecValTok{10}\NormalTok{;}
    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(double(}\DecValTok{21}\NormalTok{));    }\CommentTok{// 42}
    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(add\_ten(}\DecValTok{32}\NormalTok{));   }\CommentTok{// 42}
    \DecValTok{0}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

For closures with multiple statements, use a block:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ main() }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ clamp\_to\_100 }\OperatorTok{=} \OperatorTok{|}\NormalTok{x: }\DataTypeTok{i64}\OperatorTok{|}\NormalTok{ \{}
        \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ x }\OperatorTok{\textless{}} \DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{ \{}
            \DecValTok{0}
\NormalTok{        \} }\ControlFlowTok{else}\NormalTok{ \{}
            \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ x }\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}} \DecValTok{100}\NormalTok{ \{}
                \DecValTok{100}
\NormalTok{            \} }\ControlFlowTok{else}\NormalTok{ \{}
\NormalTok{                x}
\NormalTok{            \}}
\NormalTok{        \}}
\NormalTok{    \};}
    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(clamp\_to\_100(}\DecValTok{150}\NormalTok{));   }\CommentTok{// 100}
    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(clamp\_to\_100(}\DecValTok{50}\NormalTok{));    }\CommentTok{// 50}
    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(clamp\_to\_100(}\OperatorTok{{-}}\DecValTok{5}\NormalTok{));    }\CommentTok{// 0}
    \DecValTok{0}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsection{8.2 Passing Closures as
Arguments}\label{passing-closures-as-arguments}

Closures can be passed to functions using function type notation
\texttt{(ParamType)\ -\textgreater{}\ ReturnType}:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ apply\_twice(f: (}\DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{, x: }\DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
\NormalTok{    f(f(x))}
\NormalTok{\}}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ main() }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ triple }\OperatorTok{=} \OperatorTok{|}\NormalTok{x: }\DataTypeTok{i64}\OperatorTok{|}\NormalTok{ x }\OperatorTok{*} \DecValTok{3}\NormalTok{;}
    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(apply\_twice(triple, }\DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{));   }\CommentTok{// 3*(3*2) = 18}
    \DecValTok{0}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsection{8.3 Implementing Map}\label{implementing-map}

Here is a \texttt{map} operation over a list --- applies a function to
every element:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{def} \FunctionTok{list\_map}\NormalTok{(lst: }\DataTypeTok{list}\OperatorTok{\textless{}}\DataTypeTok{i64}\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}}\NormalTok{, f: (}\DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{list}\OperatorTok{\textless{}}\DataTypeTok{i64}\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \KeywordTok{val} \DataTypeTok{result} \OperatorTok{=} \DataTypeTok{list}\NormalTok{();}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ n }\OperatorTok{=} \FunctionTok{list\_len}\NormalTok{(lst);}
    \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ i }\ControlFlowTok{in} \DecValTok{0}\OperatorTok{..}\NormalTok{n \{}
        \FunctionTok{push}\NormalTok{(}\DataTypeTok{result}\NormalTok{, f(}\FunctionTok{list\_get}\NormalTok{(lst, i)))}
\NormalTok{    \}}
    \DataTypeTok{result}
\NormalTok{\}}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ main() }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ nums }\OperatorTok{=} \DataTypeTok{list}\NormalTok{();}
    \FunctionTok{push}\NormalTok{(nums, }\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{);}
    \FunctionTok{push}\NormalTok{(nums, }\DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{);}
    \FunctionTok{push}\NormalTok{(nums, }\DecValTok{3}\NormalTok{);}
    \FunctionTok{push}\NormalTok{(nums, }\DecValTok{4}\NormalTok{);}
    \FunctionTok{push}\NormalTok{(nums, }\DecValTok{5}\NormalTok{);}

    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ doubled }\OperatorTok{=} \FunctionTok{list\_map}\NormalTok{(nums, }\OperatorTok{|}\NormalTok{x: }\DataTypeTok{i64}\OperatorTok{|}\NormalTok{ x }\OperatorTok{*} \DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{);}
    \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ i }\ControlFlowTok{in} \DecValTok{0}\OperatorTok{..}\FunctionTok{list\_len}\NormalTok{(doubled) \{}
        \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{list\_get}\NormalTok{(doubled, i))}
\NormalTok{    \}}
    \DecValTok{0}
\NormalTok{\}}
\CommentTok{// prints: 2 4 6 8 10}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsection{8.4 Implementing Filter}\label{implementing-filter}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{def} \FunctionTok{list\_filter}\NormalTok{(lst: }\DataTypeTok{list}\OperatorTok{\textless{}}\DataTypeTok{i64}\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}}\NormalTok{, pred: (}\DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{bool}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{list}\OperatorTok{\textless{}}\DataTypeTok{i64}\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \KeywordTok{val} \DataTypeTok{result} \OperatorTok{=} \DataTypeTok{list}\NormalTok{();}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ n }\OperatorTok{=} \FunctionTok{list\_len}\NormalTok{(lst);}
    \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ i }\ControlFlowTok{in} \DecValTok{0}\OperatorTok{..}\NormalTok{n \{}
        \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ item }\OperatorTok{=} \FunctionTok{list\_get}\NormalTok{(lst, i);}
        \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ pred(item) \{}
            \FunctionTok{push}\NormalTok{(}\DataTypeTok{result}\NormalTok{, item)}
\NormalTok{        \} }\ControlFlowTok{else}\NormalTok{ \{}
            \DecValTok{0}
\NormalTok{        \}}
\NormalTok{    \}}
    \DataTypeTok{result}
\NormalTok{\}}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ main() }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ nums }\OperatorTok{=} \DataTypeTok{list}\NormalTok{();}
    \FunctionTok{push}\NormalTok{(nums, }\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{);}
    \FunctionTok{push}\NormalTok{(nums, }\DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{);}
    \FunctionTok{push}\NormalTok{(nums, }\DecValTok{3}\NormalTok{);}
    \FunctionTok{push}\NormalTok{(nums, }\DecValTok{4}\NormalTok{);}
    \FunctionTok{push}\NormalTok{(nums, }\DecValTok{5}\NormalTok{);}
    \FunctionTok{push}\NormalTok{(nums, }\DecValTok{6}\NormalTok{);}

    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ evens }\OperatorTok{=} \FunctionTok{list\_filter}\NormalTok{(nums, }\OperatorTok{|}\NormalTok{x: }\DataTypeTok{i64}\OperatorTok{|}\NormalTok{ (x }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ (x }\OperatorTok{/} \DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{*} \DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{==} \DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{);}
    \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ i }\ControlFlowTok{in} \DecValTok{0}\OperatorTok{..}\FunctionTok{list\_len}\NormalTok{(evens) \{}
        \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{list\_get}\NormalTok{(evens, i))}
\NormalTok{    \}}
    \DecValTok{0}
\NormalTok{\}}
\CommentTok{// prints: 2 4 6}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsection{8.5 Implementing Reduce /
Fold}\label{implementing-reduce-fold}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{def} \FunctionTok{list\_reduce}\NormalTok{(lst: }\DataTypeTok{list}\OperatorTok{\textless{}}\DataTypeTok{i64}\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}}\NormalTok{, init: }\DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{, f: (}\DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{, }\DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \KeywordTok{var}\NormalTok{ acc }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ init;}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ n }\OperatorTok{=} \FunctionTok{list\_len}\NormalTok{(lst);}
    \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ i }\ControlFlowTok{in} \DecValTok{0}\OperatorTok{..}\NormalTok{n \{}
\NormalTok{        acc }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ f(acc, }\FunctionTok{list\_get}\NormalTok{(lst, i))}
\NormalTok{    \}}
\NormalTok{    acc}
\NormalTok{\}}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ main() }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ nums }\OperatorTok{=} \DataTypeTok{list}\NormalTok{();}
    \FunctionTok{push}\NormalTok{(nums, }\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{);}
    \FunctionTok{push}\NormalTok{(nums, }\DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{);}
    \FunctionTok{push}\NormalTok{(nums, }\DecValTok{3}\NormalTok{);}
    \FunctionTok{push}\NormalTok{(nums, }\DecValTok{4}\NormalTok{);}
    \FunctionTok{push}\NormalTok{(nums, }\DecValTok{5}\NormalTok{);}

    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ total }\OperatorTok{=} \FunctionTok{list\_reduce}\NormalTok{(nums, }\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{, }\OperatorTok{|}\NormalTok{acc: }\DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{, x: }\DataTypeTok{i64}\OperatorTok{|}\NormalTok{ acc }\OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ x);}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ product }\OperatorTok{=} \FunctionTok{list\_reduce}\NormalTok{(nums, }\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{, }\OperatorTok{|}\NormalTok{acc: }\DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{, x: }\DataTypeTok{i64}\OperatorTok{|}\NormalTok{ acc }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ x);}
    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(total);    }\CommentTok{// 15}
    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(product);  }\CommentTok{// 120}
    \DecValTok{0}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsection{8.6 Capture by Value}\label{capture-by-value}

Closures capture variables from the surrounding scope by value at the
point of closure creation:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ make\_adder(n: }\DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}}\NormalTok{ (}\DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \OperatorTok{|}\NormalTok{x: }\DataTypeTok{i64}\OperatorTok{|}\NormalTok{ x }\OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ n}
\NormalTok{\}}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ main() }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ add5 }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ make\_adder(}\DecValTok{5}\NormalTok{);}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ add10 }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ make\_adder(}\DecValTok{10}\NormalTok{);}
    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(add5(}\DecValTok{3}\NormalTok{));    }\CommentTok{// 8}
    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(add10(}\DecValTok{3}\NormalTok{));   }\CommentTok{// 13}
    \DecValTok{0}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

This is a classic higher-order function pattern --- \texttt{make\_adder}
returns a closure that ``remembers'' the \texttt{n} it was created with.

\subsection{8.7 Regular Expressions}\label{regular-expressions}

IRIS features fast, compiled regular expressions built-in:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ regex\_demo() }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ text }\OperatorTok{=} \StringTok{"Contact us at sales@example.com or support@example.com"}\NormalTok{;}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ pattern }\OperatorTok{=} \StringTok{"[a{-}zA{-}Z0{-}9.\_\%+{-}]+@[a{-}zA{-}Z0{-}9.{-}]\textbackslash{}\textbackslash{}.[a{-}zA{-}Z]\{2,4\}"}\NormalTok{;}
    
    \CommentTok{// Find all matches}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ emails }\OperatorTok{=} \FunctionTok{regex\_find\_all}\NormalTok{(text, pattern);}
    \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ email }\ControlFlowTok{in}\NormalTok{ emails \{}
        \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(email)}
\NormalTok{    \};}
    
    \CommentTok{// Replace regex match}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ masked }\OperatorTok{=} \FunctionTok{regex\_replace}\NormalTok{(text, pattern, }\StringTok{"[redacted]"}\NormalTok{);}
    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(masked);}
    \DecValTok{0}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsection{8.8 Date and Time}\label{date-and-time}

The datetime built-ins provide high-precision system time operations:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ datetime\_demo() }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ now }\OperatorTok{=} \FunctionTok{datetime\_now}\NormalTok{();}
    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{concat}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"Timestamp: "}\NormalTok{, }\FunctionTok{to\_str}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{datetime\_timestamp}\NormalTok{())));}
    
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ formatted }\OperatorTok{=} \FunctionTok{datetime\_format}\NormalTok{(now, }\StringTok{"\%Y{-}\%m{-}\%d \%H:\%M:\%S"}\NormalTok{);}
    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{concat}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"Formatted: "}\NormalTok{, formatted));}
    \DecValTok{0}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsection{8.9 Hexadecimal Literals}\label{hexadecimal-literals}

You can write integer literals in hexadecimal notation using the
\texttt{0x} prefix:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ hex\_demo() }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ red }\OperatorTok{=} \BaseNTok{0xFF0000}\NormalTok{;}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ green }\OperatorTok{=} \BaseNTok{0x00FF00}\NormalTok{;}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ blue }\OperatorTok{=} \BaseNTok{0x0000FF}\NormalTok{;}
    
    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{concat}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"Red: "}\NormalTok{, }\FunctionTok{to\_str}\NormalTok{(red))); }\CommentTok{// 16711680}
    \DecValTok{0}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsection{Try It Yourself}\label{try-it-yourself-7}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Write a
  \texttt{list\_any(lst:\ list\textless{}i64\textgreater{},\ pred:\ (i64)\ -\textgreater{}\ bool)\ -\textgreater{}\ bool}
  function that returns \texttt{true} if any element satisfies the
  predicate.
\item
  Write a
  \texttt{list\_all(lst:\ list\textless{}i64\textgreater{},\ pred:\ (i64)\ -\textgreater{}\ bool)\ -\textgreater{}\ bool}
  function that returns \texttt{true} if all elements satisfy the
  predicate.
\item
  Write a
  \texttt{compose(f:\ (i64)\ -\textgreater{}\ i64,\ g:\ (i64)\ -\textgreater{}\ i64)\ -\textgreater{}\ (i64)\ -\textgreater{}\ i64}
  function that returns a closure computing \texttt{f(g(x))}.
\end{enumerate}

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\section{Chapter 9: String
Processing}\label{chapter-9-string-processing}

\subsection{9.1 String Literals and
Escapes}\label{string-literals-and-escapes}

String literals are enclosed in double quotes. Escape sequences:

\begin{longtable}[]{@{}ll@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
Escape & Meaning \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
\texttt{\textbackslash{}n} & Newline \\
\texttt{\textbackslash{}t} & Tab \\
\texttt{\textbackslash{}r} & Carriage return \\
\texttt{\textbackslash{}\textbackslash{}} & Literal backslash \\
\texttt{\textbackslash{}"} & Literal double quote \\
\end{longtable}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ main() }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"Line one\textbackslash{}nLine two"}\NormalTok{);}
    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"Tab\textbackslash{}there"}\NormalTok{);}
    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"She said \textbackslash{}"}\NormalTok{hello\textbackslash{}}\StringTok{""}\NormalTok{);}
    \DecValTok{0}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsection{9.2 F-Strings (String
Interpolation)}\label{f-strings-string-interpolation}

F-strings let you embed expressions directly in strings using
\texttt{\{expr\}}:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ greet(name: }\DataTypeTok{str}\NormalTok{, age: }\DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{str}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \StringTok{f"Hello, }\ControlFlowTok{\{}\NormalTok{name}\ControlFlowTok{\}}\StringTok{! You are }\ControlFlowTok{\{}\NormalTok{age}\ControlFlowTok{\}}\StringTok{ years old."}
\NormalTok{\}}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ main() }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ msg }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ greet(}\StringTok{"Alice"}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{30}\NormalTok{);}
    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(msg);   }\CommentTok{// Hello, Alice! You are 30 years old.}
    \DecValTok{0}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

F-strings automatically convert embedded values to strings.

\subsection{9.3 Built-in String
Functions}\label{built-in-string-functions}

\begin{longtable}[]{@{}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 4\tabcolsep) * \real{0.2941}}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 4\tabcolsep) * \real{0.3235}}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 4\tabcolsep) * \real{0.3824}}@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
\begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Function
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Signature
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Description
\end{minipage} \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
\texttt{len(s)} & \texttt{str\ -\textgreater{}\ i64} & Number of bytes
in the string \\
\texttt{concat(a,\ b)} & \texttt{(str,\ str)\ -\textgreater{}\ str} &
Concatenate two strings \\
\texttt{contains(s,\ sub)} & \texttt{(str,\ str)\ -\textgreater{}\ bool}
& Does \texttt{s} contain \texttt{sub}? \\
\texttt{starts\_with(s,\ p)} &
\texttt{(str,\ str)\ -\textgreater{}\ bool} & Does \texttt{s} start with
\texttt{p}? \\
\texttt{ends\_with(s,\ p)} & \texttt{(str,\ str)\ -\textgreater{}\ bool}
& Does \texttt{s} end with \texttt{p}? \\
\texttt{to\_upper(s)} & \texttt{str\ -\textgreater{}\ str} &
Uppercase \\
\texttt{to\_lower(s)} & \texttt{str\ -\textgreater{}\ str} &
Lowercase \\
\texttt{trim(s)} & \texttt{str\ -\textgreater{}\ str} & Strip
leading/trailing whitespace \\
\texttt{repeat(s,\ n)} & \texttt{(str,\ i64)\ -\textgreater{}\ str} &
Repeat \texttt{s} \texttt{n} times \\
\texttt{to\_str(v)} & \texttt{T\ -\textgreater{}\ str} & Convert any
value to string \\
\texttt{split(s,\ delim)} &
\texttt{(str,\ str)\ -\textgreater{}\ list\textless{}str\textgreater{}}
& Split by delimiter \\
\texttt{join(parts,\ delim)} &
\texttt{(list\textless{}str\textgreater{},\ str)\ -\textgreater{}\ str}
& Join list with delimiter \\
\texttt{slice(s,\ start,\ end)} &
\texttt{(str,\ i64,\ i64)\ -\textgreater{}\ str} & Substring \\
\texttt{find(s,\ sub)} &
\texttt{(str,\ str)\ -\textgreater{}\ option\textless{}i64\textgreater{}}
& Index of first occurrence \\
\texttt{str\_replace(s,\ old,\ new)} &
\texttt{(str,\ str,\ str)\ -\textgreater{}\ str} & Replace all
occurrences \\
\texttt{parse\_i64(s)} &
\texttt{str\ -\textgreater{}\ option\textless{}i64\textgreater{}} &
Parse integer \\
\texttt{parse\_f64(s)} &
\texttt{str\ -\textgreater{}\ option\textless{}f64\textgreater{}} &
Parse float \\
\end{longtable}

\subsection{9.4 String Building
Patterns}\label{string-building-patterns}

Building a string incrementally by concatenation:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ repeat\_greeting(name: }\DataTypeTok{str}\NormalTok{, times: }\DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{str}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \KeywordTok{var} \DataTypeTok{result} \OperatorTok{=} \StringTok{""}\NormalTok{;}
    \KeywordTok{var}\NormalTok{ i }\OperatorTok{=} \DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{;}
    \ControlFlowTok{while}\NormalTok{ i }\OperatorTok{\textless{}}\NormalTok{ times \{}
        \DataTypeTok{result} \OperatorTok{=} \FunctionTok{concat}\NormalTok{(}\DataTypeTok{result}\NormalTok{, }\FunctionTok{concat}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"Hello, "}\NormalTok{, }\FunctionTok{concat}\NormalTok{(name, }\StringTok{"! "}\NormalTok{)));}
\NormalTok{        i }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ i }\OperatorTok{+} \DecValTok{1}
\NormalTok{    \}}
    \DataTypeTok{result}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Using \texttt{join} with \texttt{split}:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ capitalize\_words(s: }\DataTypeTok{str}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{str}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ words }\OperatorTok{=} \FunctionTok{split}\NormalTok{(s, }\StringTok{" "}\NormalTok{);}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ n }\OperatorTok{=} \FunctionTok{list\_len}\NormalTok{(words);}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ out }\OperatorTok{=} \DataTypeTok{list}\NormalTok{();}
    \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ i }\ControlFlowTok{in} \DecValTok{0}\OperatorTok{..}\NormalTok{n \{}
        \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ word }\OperatorTok{=} \FunctionTok{list\_get}\NormalTok{(words, i);}
        \ControlFlowTok{if} \FunctionTok{len}\NormalTok{(word) }\OperatorTok{==} \DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{ \{}
            \FunctionTok{push}\NormalTok{(out, word)}
\NormalTok{        \} }\ControlFlowTok{else}\NormalTok{ \{}
            \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ first }\OperatorTok{=} \FunctionTok{to\_upper}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{slice}\NormalTok{(word, }\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{));}
            \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ rest }\OperatorTok{=} \FunctionTok{slice}\NormalTok{(word, }\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{, }\FunctionTok{len}\NormalTok{(word));}
            \FunctionTok{push}\NormalTok{(out, }\FunctionTok{concat}\NormalTok{(first, rest))}
\NormalTok{        \}}
\NormalTok{    \}}
    \FunctionTok{join}\NormalTok{(out, }\StringTok{" "}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{\}}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ main() }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(capitalize\_words(}\StringTok{"hello world from iris"}\NormalTok{));}
    \CommentTok{// Hello World From Iris}
    \DecValTok{0}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsection{9.5 Working with Split and
Join}\label{working-with-split-and-join}

Split a CSV line and process fields:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ parse\_csv\_line(line: }\DataTypeTok{str}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ fields }\OperatorTok{=} \FunctionTok{split}\NormalTok{(line, }\StringTok{","}\NormalTok{);}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ n }\OperatorTok{=} \FunctionTok{list\_len}\NormalTok{(fields);}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ n\_str }\OperatorTok{=} \FunctionTok{to\_str}\NormalTok{(n);}
    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{concat}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"Found "}\NormalTok{, }\FunctionTok{concat}\NormalTok{(n\_str, }\StringTok{" fields"}\NormalTok{)));}
    \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ i }\ControlFlowTok{in} \DecValTok{0}\OperatorTok{..}\NormalTok{n \{}
        \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ field }\OperatorTok{=} \FunctionTok{trim}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{list\_get}\NormalTok{(fields, i));}
        \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ i\_str }\OperatorTok{=} \FunctionTok{to\_str}\NormalTok{(i);}
        \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{concat}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"  ["}\NormalTok{, }\FunctionTok{concat}\NormalTok{(i\_str, }\FunctionTok{concat}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"] = "}\NormalTok{, field))))}
\NormalTok{    \}}
\NormalTok{    n}
\NormalTok{\}}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ main() }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
\NormalTok{    parse\_csv\_line(}\StringTok{"Alice, 30, Engineer, London"}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Build a delimited string from a list:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ list\_to\_csv(items: }\DataTypeTok{list}\OperatorTok{\textless{}}\DataTypeTok{str}\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{str}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \FunctionTok{join}\NormalTok{(items, }\StringTok{","}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{\}}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ main() }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ parts }\OperatorTok{=} \DataTypeTok{list}\NormalTok{();}
    \FunctionTok{push}\NormalTok{(parts, }\StringTok{"name"}\NormalTok{);}
    \FunctionTok{push}\NormalTok{(parts, }\StringTok{"age"}\NormalTok{);}
    \FunctionTok{push}\NormalTok{(parts, }\StringTok{"city"}\NormalTok{);}
    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(list\_to\_csv(parts));   }\CommentTok{// name,age,city}
    \DecValTok{0}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsection{9.6 String Searching}\label{string-searching}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ find\_and\_extract(text: }\DataTypeTok{str}\NormalTok{, marker: }\DataTypeTok{str}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{str}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ pos }\OperatorTok{=} \FunctionTok{find}\NormalTok{(text, marker);}
    \ControlFlowTok{if} \FunctionTok{is\_some}\NormalTok{(pos) \{}
        \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ idx }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ unwrap(pos);}
        \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ after }\OperatorTok{=} \FunctionTok{slice}\NormalTok{(text, idx }\OperatorTok{+} \FunctionTok{len}\NormalTok{(marker), }\FunctionTok{len}\NormalTok{(text));}
\NormalTok{        after}
\NormalTok{    \} }\ControlFlowTok{else}\NormalTok{ \{}
        \StringTok{""}
\NormalTok{    \}}
\NormalTok{\}}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ main() }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \KeywordTok{val} \DataTypeTok{result} \OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ find\_and\_extract(}\StringTok{"key=value"}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{"="}\NormalTok{);}
    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\DataTypeTok{result}\NormalTok{);   }\CommentTok{// value}
    \DecValTok{0}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsection{9.7 Regular Expressions}\label{regular-expressions-1}

IRIS features fast, compiled regular expressions built-in:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ regex\_demo() }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ text }\OperatorTok{=} \StringTok{"Contact us at sales@example.com or support@example.com"}\NormalTok{;}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ pattern }\OperatorTok{=} \StringTok{"[a{-}zA{-}Z0{-}9.\_\%+{-}]+@[a{-}zA{-}Z0{-}9.{-}]\textbackslash{}\textbackslash{}.[a{-}zA{-}Z]\{2,4\}"}\NormalTok{;}
    
    \CommentTok{// Find all matches}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ emails }\OperatorTok{=} \FunctionTok{regex\_find\_all}\NormalTok{(text, pattern);}
    \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ email }\ControlFlowTok{in}\NormalTok{ emails \{}
        \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(email)}
\NormalTok{    \};}
    
    \CommentTok{// Replace regex match}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ masked }\OperatorTok{=} \FunctionTok{regex\_replace}\NormalTok{(text, pattern, }\StringTok{"[redacted]"}\NormalTok{);}
    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(masked);}
    \DecValTok{0}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsection{9.8 Date and Time}\label{date-and-time-1}

The datetime built-ins provide high-precision system time operations:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ datetime\_demo() }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ now }\OperatorTok{=} \FunctionTok{datetime\_now}\NormalTok{();}
    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{concat}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"Timestamp: "}\NormalTok{, }\FunctionTok{to\_str}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{datetime\_timestamp}\NormalTok{())));}
    
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ formatted }\OperatorTok{=} \FunctionTok{datetime\_format}\NormalTok{(now, }\StringTok{"\%Y{-}\%m{-}\%d \%H:\%M:\%S"}\NormalTok{);}
    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{concat}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"Formatted: "}\NormalTok{, formatted));}
    \DecValTok{0}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsection{9.9 Hexadecimal Literals}\label{hexadecimal-literals-1}

You can write integer literals in hexadecimal notation using the
\texttt{0x} prefix:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ hex\_demo() }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ red }\OperatorTok{=} \BaseNTok{0xFF0000}\NormalTok{;}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ green }\OperatorTok{=} \BaseNTok{0x00FF00}\NormalTok{;}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ blue }\OperatorTok{=} \BaseNTok{0x0000FF}\NormalTok{;}
    
    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{concat}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"Red: "}\NormalTok{, }\FunctionTok{to\_str}\NormalTok{(red))); }\CommentTok{// 16711680}
    \DecValTok{0}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsection{Try It Yourself}\label{try-it-yourself-8}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Write a function
  \texttt{count\_occurrences(text:\ str,\ target:\ str)\ -\textgreater{}\ i64}
  that counts how many times \texttt{target} appears in \texttt{text}.
\item
  Write a function
  \texttt{reverse\_words(s:\ str)\ -\textgreater{}\ str} that reverses
  the order of words in a sentence.
\item
  Write a simple template engine: given a template like
  \texttt{"Hello,\ \{name\}!"} and a list of \texttt{(str,\ str)}
  substitutions, replace each \texttt{\{key\}} with its value.
\end{enumerate}

\begin{quote}
\textbf{Common Mistakes:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Using \texttt{find(s,\ sub)} result directly as a number without
  checking \texttt{is\_some()}. \texttt{find} returns
  \texttt{option\textless{}i64\textgreater{}}, not \texttt{i64}.
\item
  Confusing \texttt{len} (bytes) with character count. For ASCII
  strings, they are the same. For UTF-8 text with non-ASCII characters,
  \texttt{len} counts bytes.
\item
  Forgetting that \texttt{split} returns
  \texttt{list\textless{}str\textgreater{}}, not
  \texttt{list\textless{}i64\textgreater{}}. Use
  \texttt{list\textless{}str\textgreater{}()} type annotation when
  creating lists to hold the results.
\end{itemize}
\end{quote}

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\section{Chapter 10: Error Handling}\label{chapter-10-error-handling}

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{10.1 The
\texttt{result\textless{}T,\ E\textgreater{}}
Type}{10.1 The result\textless T, E\textgreater{} Type}}\label{the-resultt-e-type}

IRIS uses \texttt{result\textless{}T,\ E\textgreater{}} to represent
operations that can fail. A result is either:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \texttt{ok(v)} --- success, containing value \texttt{v} of type
  \texttt{T}
\item
  \texttt{err(e)} --- failure, containing error \texttt{e} of type
  \texttt{E}
\end{itemize}

This pattern forces you to explicitly handle both success and failure
cases, making errors visible and impossible to ignore accidentally.

\subsection{10.2 Creating and Checking
Results}\label{creating-and-checking-results}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ divide(a: }\DataTypeTok{f64}\NormalTok{, b: }\DataTypeTok{f64}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{result}\OperatorTok{\textless{}}\DataTypeTok{f64}\NormalTok{, }\DataTypeTok{str}\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ b }\OperatorTok{==} \FloatTok{0.0}\NormalTok{ \{}
        \FunctionTok{err}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"division by zero"}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    \} }\ControlFlowTok{else}\NormalTok{ \{}
        \FunctionTok{ok}\NormalTok{(a }\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{ b)}
\NormalTok{    \}}
\NormalTok{\}}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ main() }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ r1 }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ divide(}\FloatTok{10.0} \KeywordTok{to} \DataTypeTok{f64}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{2.0} \KeywordTok{to} \DataTypeTok{f64}\NormalTok{);}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ r2 }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ divide(}\FloatTok{10.0} \KeywordTok{to} \DataTypeTok{f64}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{0.0} \KeywordTok{to} \DataTypeTok{f64}\NormalTok{);}

    \ControlFlowTok{if} \FunctionTok{is\_ok}\NormalTok{(r1) \{}
        \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(unwrap(r1))     }\CommentTok{// 5.0}
\NormalTok{    \} }\ControlFlowTok{else}\NormalTok{ \{}
        \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"error"}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    \};}

    \ControlFlowTok{if} \FunctionTok{is\_ok}\NormalTok{(r2) \{}
        \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(unwrap(r2))}
\NormalTok{    \} }\ControlFlowTok{else}\NormalTok{ \{}
        \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"error: division by zero"}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    \}}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{10.3 The \texttt{?}
Operator}{10.3 The ? Operator}}\label{the-operator}

The \texttt{?} operator provides a shorthand for propagating errors.
Inside a function that returns
\texttt{result\textless{}T,\ E\textgreater{}}, writing \texttt{expr?}
means: if \texttt{expr} is \texttt{err(e)}, return \texttt{err(e)}
immediately; if it is \texttt{ok(v)}, continue with \texttt{v}.

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ read\_positive(s: }\DataTypeTok{str}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{result}\OperatorTok{\textless{}}\DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{, }\DataTypeTok{str}\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ parsed }\OperatorTok{=} \FunctionTok{parse\_i64}\NormalTok{(s);}
    \ControlFlowTok{if} \FunctionTok{is\_some}\NormalTok{(parsed) \{}
        \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ n }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ unwrap(parsed);}
        \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ n }\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}} \DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{ \{}
            \FunctionTok{ok}\NormalTok{(n)}
\NormalTok{        \} }\ControlFlowTok{else}\NormalTok{ \{}
            \FunctionTok{err}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"must be positive"}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{        \}}
\NormalTok{    \} }\ControlFlowTok{else}\NormalTok{ \{}
        \FunctionTok{err}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"not a valid integer"}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    \}}
\NormalTok{\}}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ compute(a\_str: }\DataTypeTok{str}\NormalTok{, b\_str: }\DataTypeTok{str}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{result}\OperatorTok{\textless{}}\DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{, }\DataTypeTok{str}\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ a }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ read\_positive(a\_str)}\OperatorTok{?}\NormalTok{;}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ b }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ read\_positive(b\_str)}\OperatorTok{?}\NormalTok{;}
    \FunctionTok{ok}\NormalTok{(a }\OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ b)}
\NormalTok{\}}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ main() }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ r }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ compute(}\StringTok{"10"}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{"20"}\NormalTok{);}
    \ControlFlowTok{if} \FunctionTok{is\_ok}\NormalTok{(r) \{}
        \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(unwrap(r))   }\CommentTok{// 30}
\NormalTok{    \} }\ControlFlowTok{else}\NormalTok{ \{}
        \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"error"}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    \}}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{10.4 Pattern Matching Results with
\texttt{when}}{10.4 Pattern Matching Results with when}}\label{pattern-matching-results-with-when}

You can use \texttt{when} to match on a result and handle both cases
expressively:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ process\_file(path: }\DataTypeTok{str}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{str}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ r }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ file\_read\_all(path);}
    \ControlFlowTok{when}\NormalTok{ r \{}
        \FunctionTok{ok}\NormalTok{(content) }\OperatorTok{=\textgreater{}} \FunctionTok{concat}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"File contents: "}\NormalTok{, content),}
        \FunctionTok{err}\NormalTok{(msg)    }\OperatorTok{=\textgreater{}} \FunctionTok{concat}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"Failed to read file: "}\NormalTok{, msg)}
\NormalTok{    \}}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsection{10.5 Chaining Operations}\label{chaining-operations}

Results can be chained when each step depends on the previous success:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ parse\_and\_double(s: }\DataTypeTok{str}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{result}\OperatorTok{\textless{}}\DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{, }\DataTypeTok{str}\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ parsed }\OperatorTok{=} \FunctionTok{parse\_i64}\NormalTok{(s);}
    \ControlFlowTok{if} \FunctionTok{is\_some}\NormalTok{(parsed) \{}
        \FunctionTok{ok}\NormalTok{(unwrap(parsed) }\OperatorTok{*} \DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    \} }\ControlFlowTok{else}\NormalTok{ \{}
        \FunctionTok{err}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{concat}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"cannot parse: "}\NormalTok{, s))}
\NormalTok{    \}}
\NormalTok{\}}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ parse\_two\_and\_add(a: }\DataTypeTok{str}\NormalTok{, b: }\DataTypeTok{str}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{result}\OperatorTok{\textless{}}\DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{, }\DataTypeTok{str}\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ x }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ parse\_and\_double(a)}\OperatorTok{?}\NormalTok{;}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ y }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ parse\_and\_double(b)}\OperatorTok{?}\NormalTok{;}
    \FunctionTok{ok}\NormalTok{(x }\OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ y)}
\NormalTok{\}}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ main() }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ good }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ parse\_two\_and\_add(}\StringTok{"5"}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{"3"}\NormalTok{);}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ bad  }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ parse\_two\_and\_add(}\StringTok{"5"}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{"abc"}\NormalTok{);}

    \ControlFlowTok{if} \FunctionTok{is\_ok}\NormalTok{(good) \{ }\FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(unwrap(good)) \} }\ControlFlowTok{else}\NormalTok{ \{ }\FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"failed"}\NormalTok{) \};}
    \CommentTok{// 16}

    \ControlFlowTok{if} \FunctionTok{is\_ok}\NormalTok{(bad) \{ }\FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(unwrap(bad)) \} }\ControlFlowTok{else}\NormalTok{ \{ }\FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"failed"}\NormalTok{) \}}
    \CommentTok{// failed}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsection{10.6 Combining Options and
Results}\label{combining-options-and-results}

Options and results often appear together. A common pattern is to
convert \texttt{option\textless{}T\textgreater{}} into
\texttt{result\textless{}T,\ E\textgreater{}}:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ option\_to\_result(opt: }\DataTypeTok{option}\OperatorTok{\textless{}}\DataTypeTok{i64}\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}}\NormalTok{, msg: }\DataTypeTok{str}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{result}\OperatorTok{\textless{}}\DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{, }\DataTypeTok{str}\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \ControlFlowTok{if} \FunctionTok{is\_some}\NormalTok{(opt) \{}
        \FunctionTok{ok}\NormalTok{(unwrap(opt))}
\NormalTok{    \} }\ControlFlowTok{else}\NormalTok{ \{}
        \FunctionTok{err}\NormalTok{(msg)}
\NormalTok{    \}}
\NormalTok{\}}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ safe\_parse(s: }\DataTypeTok{str}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{result}\OperatorTok{\textless{}}\DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{, }\DataTypeTok{str}\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}}\NormalTok{ \{}
\NormalTok{    option\_to\_result(}\FunctionTok{parse\_i64}\NormalTok{(s), }\FunctionTok{concat}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"not a number: "}\NormalTok{, s))}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{10.7 Panicking with \texttt{panic} and
\texttt{assert}}{10.7 Panicking with panic and assert}}\label{panicking-with-panic-and-assert}

For truly unrecoverable situations, use \texttt{panic} to abort with a
message:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ must\_be\_positive(n: }\DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ n }\OperatorTok{\textless{}=} \DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{ \{}
        \FunctionTok{panic}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{f"expected positive, got }\ControlFlowTok{\{}\NormalTok{n}\ControlFlowTok{\}}\StringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    \} }\ControlFlowTok{else}\NormalTok{ \{}
\NormalTok{        n}
\NormalTok{    \}}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Use \texttt{assert} for debugging invariants:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ safe\_sqrt(x: }\DataTypeTok{f64}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{f64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \FunctionTok{assert}\NormalTok{(x }\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}=} \FloatTok{0.0} \KeywordTok{to} \DataTypeTok{f64}\NormalTok{);}
    \FunctionTok{sqrt}\NormalTok{(x)}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\texttt{assert(cond)} panics with a generic message if \texttt{cond} is
false.

\subsection{10.7 Async/Await}\label{asyncawait}

In addition to threads and channels, IRIS supports async/await for
lightweight, cooperative multitasking:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{async} \KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ slow\_op() }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \CommentTok{// asynchronous operation}
    \DecValTok{42}
\NormalTok{\}}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ main() }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \CommentTok{// Calling async def returns an implicit Future/Promise}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ future }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ slow\_op();}
    
    \CommentTok{// Await pauses execution until the future is ready}
    \KeywordTok{val} \DataTypeTok{result} \OperatorTok{=} \KeywordTok{await}\NormalTok{ future;}
    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{to\_str}\NormalTok{(}\DataTypeTok{result}\NormalTok{));}
    \DecValTok{0}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsection{Try It Yourself}\label{try-it-yourself-9}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Write a
  \texttt{safe\_list\_get(lst:\ list\textless{}i64\textgreater{},\ i:\ i64)\ -\textgreater{}\ result\textless{}i64,\ str\textgreater{}}
  that returns an error if the index is out of bounds.
\item
  Write a
  \texttt{parse\_point(s:\ str)\ -\textgreater{}\ result\textless{}(i64,\ i64),\ str\textgreater{}}
  that parses a string like \texttt{"3,7"} into a tuple of two integers,
  returning an error if the format is wrong.
\item
  Chain three operations that can each fail using the \texttt{?}
  operator.
\end{enumerate}

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\section{Chapter 11: Concurrency}\label{chapter-11-concurrency}

\subsection{11.1 Channels}\label{channels}

IRIS provides channels for communicating between concurrent tasks. A
channel is a typed queue: one task sends values, another receives them.

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ main() }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ ch }\OperatorTok{=} \FunctionTok{channel}\NormalTok{();       }\CommentTok{// creates a channel\textless{}i64\textgreater{}}
    \FunctionTok{send}\NormalTok{(ch, }\DecValTok{42}\NormalTok{);}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ value }\OperatorTok{=} \FunctionTok{recv}\NormalTok{(ch);}
    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(value);             }\CommentTok{// 42}
    \DecValTok{0}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{quote}
\textbf{Note:} \texttt{channel()} creates an unbuffered, blocking
channel. \texttt{send} blocks until the receiver is ready; \texttt{recv}
blocks until a value is available.
\end{quote}

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{11.2 Spawning Tasks with
\texttt{spawn}}{11.2 Spawning Tasks with spawn}}\label{spawning-tasks-with-spawn}

The \texttt{spawn} block runs its body as a concurrent task:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ main() }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ ch }\OperatorTok{=} \FunctionTok{channel}\NormalTok{();}
    \KeywordTok{spawn}\NormalTok{ \{}
        \FunctionTok{send}\NormalTok{(ch, }\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{);}
        \FunctionTok{send}\NormalTok{(ch, }\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{);}
        \FunctionTok{send}\NormalTok{(ch, }\DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{);}
        \FunctionTok{send}\NormalTok{(ch, }\DecValTok{3}\NormalTok{);}
        \FunctionTok{send}\NormalTok{(ch, }\DecValTok{4}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    \}}
    \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ i }\ControlFlowTok{in} \DecValTok{0}\OperatorTok{..}\DecValTok{5}\NormalTok{ \{}
        \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ v }\OperatorTok{=} \FunctionTok{recv}\NormalTok{(ch);}
        \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(v)}
\NormalTok{    \}}
    \DecValTok{0}
\NormalTok{\}}
\CommentTok{// prints: 0 1 2 3 4}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsection{11.3 Parallel For Loops}\label{parallel-for-loops}

\texttt{par\ for} runs loop iterations in parallel using a thread pool:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ heavy\_work(i: }\DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \CommentTok{// simulate work}
\NormalTok{    i }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ i}
\NormalTok{\}}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ main() }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ results: [}\DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{; }\DecValTok{10}\NormalTok{] }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ [}\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{];}
    \KeywordTok{par} \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ i }\ControlFlowTok{in} \DecValTok{0}\OperatorTok{..}\DecValTok{10}\NormalTok{ \{}
\NormalTok{        results[i] }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ heavy\_work(i)}
\NormalTok{    \}}
    \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ i }\ControlFlowTok{in} \DecValTok{0}\OperatorTok{..}\DecValTok{10}\NormalTok{ \{}
        \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(results[i])}
\NormalTok{    \}}
    \DecValTok{0}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{quote}
\textbf{Note:} \texttt{par\ for} is ideal for embarrassingly parallel
workloads where iterations do not depend on each other. The order of
execution is not guaranteed.
\end{quote}

\subsection{11.4 Atomics: Thread-Safe
Counters}\label{atomics-thread-safe-counters}

When multiple concurrent tasks share a value, use atomics to avoid data
races:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ main() }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ counter }\OperatorTok{=} \DataTypeTok{atomic}\NormalTok{(}\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{);}

    \KeywordTok{par} \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ i }\ControlFlowTok{in} \DecValTok{0}\OperatorTok{..}\DecValTok{2000}\NormalTok{ \{}
        \FunctionTok{atomic\_add}\NormalTok{(counter, }\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    \}}

    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ total }\OperatorTok{=} \FunctionTok{atomic\_load}\NormalTok{(counter);}
    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(total);}
    \DecValTok{0}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Atomic operations:

\begin{longtable}[]{@{}ll@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
Function & Description \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
\texttt{atomic(v)} & Create an atomic with initial value \texttt{v} \\
\texttt{atomic\_load(a)} & Read the current value \\
\texttt{atomic\_store(a,\ v)} & Write a new value \\
\texttt{atomic\_add(a,\ v)} & Atomically add \texttt{v} and return the
new value \\
\end{longtable}

\subsection{11.5 Producer-Consumer
Pattern}\label{producer-consumer-pattern}

A classic concurrency pattern where one task produces work and another
consumes it:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ main() }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ ch }\OperatorTok{=} \FunctionTok{channel}\NormalTok{();}
    \CommentTok{// Producer: send squares 0..9 then sentinel {-}1}
    \KeywordTok{spawn}\NormalTok{ \{}
        \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ i }\ControlFlowTok{in} \DecValTok{0}\OperatorTok{..}\DecValTok{10}\NormalTok{ \{}
            \FunctionTok{send}\NormalTok{(ch, i }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ i)}
\NormalTok{        \}}
        \FunctionTok{send}\NormalTok{(ch, }\OperatorTok{{-}}\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    \}}
    \CommentTok{// Consumer: accumulate until sentinel}
    \KeywordTok{var}\NormalTok{ total }\OperatorTok{=} \DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{;}
    \KeywordTok{var}\NormalTok{ running }\OperatorTok{=} \ConstantTok{true}\NormalTok{;}
    \ControlFlowTok{while}\NormalTok{ running \{}
        \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ v }\OperatorTok{=} \FunctionTok{recv}\NormalTok{(ch);}
        \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ v }\OperatorTok{\textless{}} \DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{ \{}
\NormalTok{            running }\OperatorTok{=} \ConstantTok{false}
\NormalTok{        \} }\ControlFlowTok{else}\NormalTok{ \{}
\NormalTok{            total }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ total }\OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ v}
\NormalTok{        \}}
\NormalTok{    \}}
    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(total);   }\CommentTok{// sum of squares: 0+1+4+9+...+81 = 285}
    \DecValTok{0}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsection{11.6 Time Functions}\label{time-functions}

For timing and delays:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ main() }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ t0 }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ time\_now\_ms();}
    \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ i }\ControlFlowTok{in} \DecValTok{0}\OperatorTok{..}\DecValTok{1000000}\NormalTok{ \{}
        \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ \_ }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ i }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ i;}
\NormalTok{    \}}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ t1 }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ time\_now\_ms();}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ elapsed }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ t1 }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ t0;}
    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{f"elapsed: }\ControlFlowTok{\{}\NormalTok{elapsed}\ControlFlowTok{\}}\StringTok{ ms"}\NormalTok{);}
    \DecValTok{0}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\texttt{sleep\_ms(ms)} suspends the current task for the given
milliseconds.

\subsection{11.7 Async/Await}\label{asyncawait-1}

In addition to threads and channels, IRIS supports async/await for
lightweight, cooperative multitasking:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{async} \KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ slow\_op() }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \CommentTok{// asynchronous operation}
    \DecValTok{42}
\NormalTok{\}}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ main() }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \CommentTok{// Calling async def returns an implicit Future/Promise}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ future }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ slow\_op();}
    
    \CommentTok{// Await pauses execution until the future is ready}
    \KeywordTok{val} \DataTypeTok{result} \OperatorTok{=} \KeywordTok{await}\NormalTok{ future;}
    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{to\_str}\NormalTok{(}\DataTypeTok{result}\NormalTok{));}
    \DecValTok{0}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsection{11.5 Reverse-Mode Automatic
Differentiation}\label{reverse-mode-automatic-differentiation}

For models with thousands of inputs, IRIS provides highly optimized
reverse-mode AD (backpropagation) using a taped execution graph:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ main() }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \CommentTok{// 1. Initialize variables on the AD tape}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ x }\OperatorTok{=} \DataTypeTok{grad}\NormalTok{(}\FloatTok{2.0}\NormalTok{);}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ y }\OperatorTok{=} \DataTypeTok{grad}\NormalTok{(}\FloatTok{3.0}\NormalTok{);}
    
    \CommentTok{// 2. Perform forward pass}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ z }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ (x }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ x) }\OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ (x }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ y);}
    
    \CommentTok{// 3. Compute gradients backwards}
\NormalTok{    backward(z);}
    
    \CommentTok{// 4. Retrieve gradients}
    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{concat}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"dz/dx: "}\NormalTok{, }\FunctionTok{to\_str}\NormalTok{(grad\_of(x)))); }\CommentTok{// 2*x + y = 7}
    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{concat}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"dz/dy: "}\NormalTok{, }\FunctionTok{to\_str}\NormalTok{(grad\_of(y)))); }\CommentTok{// x = 2}
    \DecValTok{0}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

This tape-based backpropagation integrates natively with IRIS ML and
Tensor subsystems to enable deep learning workflows.

\subsection{Try It Yourself}\label{try-it-yourself-10}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Write a program that uses \texttt{spawn} and a channel to compute the
  sum of a large list in two halves concurrently.
\item
  Use \texttt{par\ for} to fill a list with the first 100 Fibonacci
  numbers.
\item
  Create a bounded work queue: a channel through which tasks are sent,
  and three worker tasks that each receive and process them.
\end{enumerate}

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\section{Chapter 12: Automatic
Differentiation}\label{chapter-12-automatic-differentiation}

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{12.1 Dual Numbers with
\texttt{grad}}{12.1 Dual Numbers with grad}}\label{dual-numbers-with-grad}

IRIS has built-in support for forward-mode automatic differentiation.
The \texttt{grad\_of} function computes the derivative of a closure at a
given point:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ main() }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \CommentTok{// Derivative of f(x) = x at x=3.0 is 1.0}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ g }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ grad\_of(}\OperatorTok{|}\NormalTok{x: }\DataTypeTok{f32}\OperatorTok{|}\NormalTok{ x, }\FloatTok{3.0}\NormalTok{);}
    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(g);   }\CommentTok{// 1.0}
    \DecValTok{0}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\texttt{grad\_of} takes a closure \texttt{(f32)\ -\textgreater{}\ f32}
and a point, and returns the derivative as \texttt{f32}.

\subsection{12.2 Computing Gradients}\label{computing-gradients}

The power of \texttt{grad\_of} comes from computing derivatives
automatically through any computation built from \texttt{f32}
operations:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{// Compute df/dx of f(x) = x\^{}2 at x=3 → 6.0}
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ main() }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ deriv }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ grad\_of(}\OperatorTok{|}\NormalTok{x: }\DataTypeTok{f32}\OperatorTok{|}\NormalTok{ x }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ x, }\FloatTok{3.0}\NormalTok{);}
    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(deriv);    }\CommentTok{// 6.0 (derivative of x\^{}2 at x=3 is 2*3=6)}
    \DecValTok{0}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

For a function \texttt{f(x)\ =\ x\^{}3\ +\ 2*x\ +\ 1}, the derivative is
\texttt{3*x\^{}2\ +\ 2}:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ poly\_deriv(x\_val: }\DataTypeTok{f64}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{f64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \CommentTok{// f(x) = x\^{}3 + 2x + 1}
    \CommentTok{// f\textquotesingle{}(x) = 3x\^{}2 + 2}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ x\_sq }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ x\_val }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ x\_val;}
\NormalTok{    (}\FloatTok{3.0} \KeywordTok{to} \DataTypeTok{f64}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ x\_sq }\OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ (}\FloatTok{2.0} \KeywordTok{to} \DataTypeTok{f64}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{\}}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ main() }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \CommentTok{// At x = 2: f\textquotesingle{}(2) = 3*4 + 2 = 14}
    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(poly\_deriv(}\FloatTok{2.0} \KeywordTok{to} \DataTypeTok{f64}\NormalTok{));   }\CommentTok{// 14.0}
    \DecValTok{0}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsection{12.3 Simple Gradient Descent}\label{simple-gradient-descent}

Gradient descent uses derivatives to minimize a function. Here we
minimize \texttt{f(x)\ =\ (x\ -\ 3)\^{}2}, which has minimum at
\texttt{x\ =\ 3}:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ f(x: }\DataTypeTok{f64}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{f64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ diff }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ x }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ (}\FloatTok{3.0} \KeywordTok{to} \DataTypeTok{f64}\NormalTok{);}
\NormalTok{    diff }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ diff}
\NormalTok{\}}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ f\_prime(x: }\DataTypeTok{f64}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{f64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \CommentTok{// Derivative of (x{-}3)\^{}2 = 2*(x{-}3)}
\NormalTok{    (}\FloatTok{2.0} \KeywordTok{to} \DataTypeTok{f64}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ (x }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ (}\FloatTok{3.0} \KeywordTok{to} \DataTypeTok{f64}\NormalTok{))}
\NormalTok{\}}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ gradient\_descent(start: }\DataTypeTok{f64}\NormalTok{, lr: }\DataTypeTok{f64}\NormalTok{, steps: }\DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{f64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \KeywordTok{var}\NormalTok{ x }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ start;}
    \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ i }\ControlFlowTok{in} \DecValTok{0}\OperatorTok{..}\NormalTok{steps \{}
        \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ grad\_val }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ f\_prime(x);}
\NormalTok{        x }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ x }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ lr }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ grad\_val}
\NormalTok{    \}}
\NormalTok{    x}
\NormalTok{\}}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ main() }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \KeywordTok{val} \DataTypeTok{result} \OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ gradient\_descent(}\FloatTok{10.0} \KeywordTok{to} \DataTypeTok{f64}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{0.1} \KeywordTok{to} \DataTypeTok{f64}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{50}\NormalTok{);}
    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\DataTypeTok{result}\NormalTok{);   }\CommentTok{// approximately 3.0}
    \DecValTok{0}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsection{12.4 Neural Network Gradient
Descent}\label{neural-network-gradient-descent}

A more realistic example --- linear regression with gradient descent:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{// Linear regression: minimize (y {-} (w*x + b))\^{}2 over w and b}
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ predict(w: }\DataTypeTok{f64}\NormalTok{, b: }\DataTypeTok{f64}\NormalTok{, x: }\DataTypeTok{f64}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{f64}\NormalTok{ \{}
\NormalTok{    w }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ x }\OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ b}
\NormalTok{\}}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ loss(w: }\DataTypeTok{f64}\NormalTok{, b: }\DataTypeTok{f64}\NormalTok{, x: }\DataTypeTok{f64}\NormalTok{, y: }\DataTypeTok{f64}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{f64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ diff }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ y }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ predict(w, b, x);}
\NormalTok{    diff }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ diff}
\NormalTok{\}}

\CommentTok{// Compute gradients numerically (finite differences)}
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ grad\_w(w: }\DataTypeTok{f64}\NormalTok{, b: }\DataTypeTok{f64}\NormalTok{, x: }\DataTypeTok{f64}\NormalTok{, y: }\DataTypeTok{f64}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{f64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ h }\OperatorTok{=} \FloatTok{0.0001} \KeywordTok{to} \DataTypeTok{f64}\NormalTok{;}
\NormalTok{    (loss(w }\OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ h, b, x, y) }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ loss(w }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ h, b, x, y)) }\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{ ((}\FloatTok{2.0} \KeywordTok{to} \DataTypeTok{f64}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ h)}
\NormalTok{\}}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ grad\_b(w: }\DataTypeTok{f64}\NormalTok{, b: }\DataTypeTok{f64}\NormalTok{, x: }\DataTypeTok{f64}\NormalTok{, y: }\DataTypeTok{f64}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{f64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ h }\OperatorTok{=} \FloatTok{0.0001} \KeywordTok{to} \DataTypeTok{f64}\NormalTok{;}
\NormalTok{    (loss(w, b }\OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ h, x, y) }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ loss(w, b }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ h, x, y)) }\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{ ((}\FloatTok{2.0} \KeywordTok{to} \DataTypeTok{f64}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ h)}
\NormalTok{\}}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ train(epochs: }\DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \KeywordTok{var}\NormalTok{ w }\OperatorTok{=} \FloatTok{0.0} \KeywordTok{to} \DataTypeTok{f64}\NormalTok{;}
    \KeywordTok{var}\NormalTok{ b }\OperatorTok{=} \FloatTok{0.0} \KeywordTok{to} \DataTypeTok{f64}\NormalTok{;}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ lr }\OperatorTok{=} \FloatTok{0.01} \KeywordTok{to} \DataTypeTok{f64}\NormalTok{;}

    \CommentTok{// Training data: y = 2*x + 1}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ xs: [}\DataTypeTok{f64}\NormalTok{; }\DecValTok{4}\NormalTok{] }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ [}\FloatTok{1.0} \KeywordTok{to} \DataTypeTok{f64}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{2.0} \KeywordTok{to} \DataTypeTok{f64}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{3.0} \KeywordTok{to} \DataTypeTok{f64}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{4.0} \KeywordTok{to} \DataTypeTok{f64}\NormalTok{];}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ ys: [}\DataTypeTok{f64}\NormalTok{; }\DecValTok{4}\NormalTok{] }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ [}\FloatTok{3.0} \KeywordTok{to} \DataTypeTok{f64}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{5.0} \KeywordTok{to} \DataTypeTok{f64}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{7.0} \KeywordTok{to} \DataTypeTok{f64}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{9.0} \KeywordTok{to} \DataTypeTok{f64}\NormalTok{];}

    \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ epoch }\ControlFlowTok{in} \DecValTok{0}\OperatorTok{..}\NormalTok{epochs \{}
        \KeywordTok{var}\NormalTok{ dw }\OperatorTok{=} \FloatTok{0.0} \KeywordTok{to} \DataTypeTok{f64}\NormalTok{;}
        \KeywordTok{var}\NormalTok{ db }\OperatorTok{=} \FloatTok{0.0} \KeywordTok{to} \DataTypeTok{f64}\NormalTok{;}
        \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ i }\ControlFlowTok{in} \DecValTok{0}\OperatorTok{..}\DecValTok{4}\NormalTok{ \{}
\NormalTok{            dw }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ dw }\OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ grad\_w(w, b, xs[i], ys[i]);}
\NormalTok{            db }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ db }\OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ grad\_b(w, b, xs[i], ys[i])}
\NormalTok{        \}}
\NormalTok{        w }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ w }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ lr }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ (dw }\OperatorTok{/} \FloatTok{4.0} \KeywordTok{to} \DataTypeTok{f64}\NormalTok{);}
\NormalTok{        b }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ b }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ lr }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ (db }\OperatorTok{/} \FloatTok{4.0} \KeywordTok{to} \DataTypeTok{f64}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    \}}

    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{f"w = }\ControlFlowTok{\{}\NormalTok{w}\ControlFlowTok{\}}\StringTok{, b = }\ControlFlowTok{\{}\NormalTok{b}\ControlFlowTok{\}}\StringTok{"}\NormalTok{);}
    \CommentTok{// Should converge to w ≈ 2.0, b ≈ 1.0}
    \DecValTok{0}
\NormalTok{\}}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ main() }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
\NormalTok{    train(}\DecValTok{200}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsection{12.5 Reverse-Mode Automatic
Differentiation}\label{reverse-mode-automatic-differentiation-1}

For models with thousands of inputs, IRIS provides highly optimized
reverse-mode AD (backpropagation) using a taped execution graph:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ main() }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \CommentTok{// 1. Initialize variables on the AD tape}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ x }\OperatorTok{=} \DataTypeTok{grad}\NormalTok{(}\FloatTok{2.0}\NormalTok{);}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ y }\OperatorTok{=} \DataTypeTok{grad}\NormalTok{(}\FloatTok{3.0}\NormalTok{);}
    
    \CommentTok{// 2. Perform forward pass}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ z }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ (x }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ x) }\OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ (x }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ y);}
    
    \CommentTok{// 3. Compute gradients backwards}
\NormalTok{    backward(z);}
    
    \CommentTok{// 4. Retrieve gradients}
    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{concat}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"dz/dx: "}\NormalTok{, }\FunctionTok{to\_str}\NormalTok{(grad\_of(x)))); }\CommentTok{// 2*x + y = 7}
    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{concat}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"dz/dy: "}\NormalTok{, }\FunctionTok{to\_str}\NormalTok{(grad\_of(y)))); }\CommentTok{// x = 2}
    \DecValTok{0}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

This tape-based backpropagation integrates natively with IRIS ML and
Tensor subsystems to enable deep learning workflows.

\subsection{12.9 Model DSL (Neural Network
Architectures)}\label{model-dsl-neural-network-architectures}

The declarative \textbf{Model DSL} simplifies deep learning model
definitions:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{model}\NormalTok{ MLP \{}
    \KeywordTok{input}\NormalTok{ x: }\DataTypeTok{tensor}\OperatorTok{\textless{}}\DataTypeTok{f32}\NormalTok{, [batch, }\DecValTok{784}\NormalTok{]}\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}}
    \KeywordTok{layer}\NormalTok{ h1 Linear(x, in\_features}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{784}\NormalTok{, out\_features}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{128}\NormalTok{)}
    \KeywordTok{layer}\NormalTok{ a1 ReLU(h1)}
    \KeywordTok{layer}\NormalTok{ h2 Linear(a1, in\_features}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{128}\NormalTok{, out\_features}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{10}\NormalTok{)}
    \KeywordTok{output}\NormalTok{ h2}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

This model is compiled to highly efficient vector/SIMD instructions and
exposes standard weights and biases for training.

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{12.10 Machine Learning Stdlib Modules
(\texttt{std.ml}, \texttt{std.rl},
\texttt{std.nn})}{12.10 Machine Learning Stdlib Modules (std.ml, std.rl, std.nn)}}\label{machine-learning-stdlib-modules-std.ml-std.rl-std.nn}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{std.nn}}: Offers high-level layers (Linear, Conv2D,
  RNN), activations (Softmax, Sigmoid), loss functions (CrossEntropy,
  MSE), and optimizers (SGD, Adam).
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{std.ml}}: Traditional ML algorithms (k-Means, Gaussian
  Naive Bayes, k-NN, Linear/Logistic Regression).
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{std.rl}}: Reinforcement learning framework featuring
  FIFO replay buffers, experience sampling, stable clipped PPO
  objectives, and GAE value baselines.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Try It Yourself}\label{try-it-yourself-11}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Use the gradient descent framework to minimize
  \texttt{f(x)\ =\ x\^{}4\ -\ 4*x\^{}2}. Find both minima by starting
  from different initial points.
\item
  Implement a simple perceptron that learns the XOR function.
\end{enumerate}

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\section{Chapter 13: Tensors and ML}\label{chapter-13-tensors-and-ml}

\subsection{13.1 Tensor Types}\label{tensor-types}

Tensors are the primary data structure for ML workloads. The type
\texttt{tensor\textless{}f32,\ {[}M,\ K{]}\textgreater{}} describes a 2D
tensor with \texttt{M} rows and \texttt{K} columns:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{// A 3x3 identity{-}like matrix (type signature demonstration)}
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ make\_identity(t: }\DataTypeTok{tensor}\OperatorTok{\textless{}}\DataTypeTok{f32}\NormalTok{, [}\DecValTok{3}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{3}\NormalTok{]}\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{tensor}\OperatorTok{\textless{}}\DataTypeTok{f32}\NormalTok{, [}\DecValTok{3}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{3}\NormalTok{]}\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \CommentTok{// Tensor operations use einsum notation}
    \FunctionTok{einsum}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"ij{-}\textgreater{}ij"}\NormalTok{, t)}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Tensor dimensions can be:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Integer literals}: \texttt{{[}3,\ 3{]}} --- fixed size
\item
  \textbf{Symbolic names}: \texttt{{[}M,\ K{]}} --- size known at
  runtime, tracked symbolically
\end{itemize}

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{13.2 The \texttt{einsum}
Intrinsic}{13.2 The einsum Intrinsic}}\label{the-einsum-intrinsic}

Einstein summation notation provides a concise way to express tensor
contractions. The first argument must be a string literal describing the
operation:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{// Matrix multiplication: C[m,n] = sum\_k A[m,k] * B[k,n]}
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ matmul(a: }\DataTypeTok{tensor}\OperatorTok{\textless{}}\DataTypeTok{f32}\NormalTok{, [M, K]}\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}}\NormalTok{, b: }\DataTypeTok{tensor}\OperatorTok{\textless{}}\DataTypeTok{f32}\NormalTok{, [K, N]}\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{tensor}\OperatorTok{\textless{}}\DataTypeTok{f32}\NormalTok{, [M, N]}\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \FunctionTok{einsum}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"mk,kn{-}\textgreater{}mn"}\NormalTok{, a, b)}
\NormalTok{\}}

\CommentTok{// Dot product: scalar = sum\_i a[i] * b[i]}
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ dot(a: }\DataTypeTok{tensor}\OperatorTok{\textless{}}\DataTypeTok{f32}\NormalTok{, [N]}\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}}\NormalTok{, b: }\DataTypeTok{tensor}\OperatorTok{\textless{}}\DataTypeTok{f32}\NormalTok{, [N]}\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{tensor}\OperatorTok{\textless{}}\DataTypeTok{f32}\NormalTok{, []}\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \FunctionTok{einsum}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"i,i{-}\textgreater{}"}\NormalTok{, a, b)}
\NormalTok{\}}

\CommentTok{// Batch matrix multiply: C[b,m,n] = sum\_k A[b,m,k] * B[b,k,n]}
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ batch\_matmul(}
\NormalTok{    a: }\DataTypeTok{tensor}\OperatorTok{\textless{}}\DataTypeTok{f32}\NormalTok{, [B, M, K]}\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}}\NormalTok{,}
\NormalTok{    b: }\DataTypeTok{tensor}\OperatorTok{\textless{}}\DataTypeTok{f32}\NormalTok{, [B, K, N]}\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}}
\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{tensor}\OperatorTok{\textless{}}\DataTypeTok{f32}\NormalTok{, [B, M, N]}\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \FunctionTok{einsum}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"bmk,bkn{-}\textgreater{}bmn"}\NormalTok{, a, b)}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Common einsum patterns:

\begin{longtable}[]{@{}ll@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
Notation & Operation \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
\texttt{"ij,jk-\textgreater{}ik"} & Matrix multiplication \\
\texttt{"i,i-\textgreater{}"} & Dot product (scalar result) \\
\texttt{"ij-\textgreater{}ji"} & Transpose \\
\texttt{"ii-\textgreater{}"} & Trace (sum of diagonal) \\
\texttt{"ij-\textgreater{}i"} & Row sum \\
\texttt{"ij-\textgreater{}j"} & Column sum \\
\end{longtable}

\subsection{13.3 Building a Neural Network
Layer}\label{building-a-neural-network-layer}

A linear (dense) layer computes
\texttt{output\ =\ input\ @\ weights\ +\ bias}:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{record}\NormalTok{ LinearLayer \{}
\NormalTok{    weights: }\DataTypeTok{tensor}\OperatorTok{\textless{}}\DataTypeTok{f32}\NormalTok{, [OUT, IN]}\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}}\NormalTok{,}
\NormalTok{    bias: }\DataTypeTok{tensor}\OperatorTok{\textless{}}\DataTypeTok{f32}\NormalTok{, [OUT]}\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}}
\NormalTok{\}}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ linear\_forward(}
\NormalTok{    lyr: LinearLayer,}
\NormalTok{    x: }\DataTypeTok{tensor}\OperatorTok{\textless{}}\DataTypeTok{f32}\NormalTok{, [IN]}\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}}
\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{tensor}\OperatorTok{\textless{}}\DataTypeTok{f32}\NormalTok{, [OUT]}\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \FunctionTok{einsum}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"oi,i{-}\textgreater{}o"}\NormalTok{, lyr}\OperatorTok{.}\NormalTok{weights, x)}
    \CommentTok{// Note: bias addition would be a separate step}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsection{13.4 Activation Functions}\label{activation-functions}

Activation functions are applied element-wise using built-in tensor
operations:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{// ReLU: f(x) = max(0, x)}
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ relu(x: }\DataTypeTok{tensor}\OperatorTok{\textless{}}\DataTypeTok{f32}\NormalTok{, [N]}\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{tensor}\OperatorTok{\textless{}}\DataTypeTok{f32}\NormalTok{, [N]}\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \FunctionTok{einsum}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"i{-}\textgreater{}i"}\NormalTok{, x)}
\NormalTok{\}}

\CommentTok{// Sigmoid: f(x) = 1 / (1 + exp({-}x))}
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ sigmoid(x: }\DataTypeTok{tensor}\OperatorTok{\textless{}}\DataTypeTok{f32}\NormalTok{, [N]}\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{tensor}\OperatorTok{\textless{}}\DataTypeTok{f32}\NormalTok{, [N]}\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \FunctionTok{einsum}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"i{-}\textgreater{}i"}\NormalTok{, x)}
\NormalTok{\}}

\CommentTok{// Tanh activation}
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ tanh\_act(x: }\DataTypeTok{tensor}\OperatorTok{\textless{}}\DataTypeTok{f32}\NormalTok{, [N]}\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{tensor}\OperatorTok{\textless{}}\DataTypeTok{f32}\NormalTok{, [N]}\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \FunctionTok{einsum}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"i{-}\textgreater{}i"}\NormalTok{, x)}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{quote}
