\textbf{Note:} In practice, IRIS applies activation functions via
runtime kernels. The \texttt{einsum} identity pass-through shown here
demonstrates the type signatures. The compiler recognizes well-known
activation patterns and emits optimized code.
\end{quote}

\subsection{13.5 A Simple Training Loop}\label{a-simple-training-loop}

Putting it together with a minimal training loop structure:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{// Two{-}layer MLP for classification}
\KeywordTok{record}\NormalTok{ MLP \{}
\NormalTok{    w1: }\DataTypeTok{tensor}\OperatorTok{\textless{}}\DataTypeTok{f32}\NormalTok{, [H, IN]}\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}}\NormalTok{,}
\NormalTok{    b1: }\DataTypeTok{tensor}\OperatorTok{\textless{}}\DataTypeTok{f32}\NormalTok{, [H]}\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}}\NormalTok{,}
\NormalTok{    w2: }\DataTypeTok{tensor}\OperatorTok{\textless{}}\DataTypeTok{f32}\NormalTok{, [OUT, H]}\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}}\NormalTok{,}
\NormalTok{    b2: }\DataTypeTok{tensor}\OperatorTok{\textless{}}\DataTypeTok{f32}\NormalTok{, [OUT]}\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}}
\NormalTok{\}}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ forward(mdl: MLP, x: }\DataTypeTok{tensor}\OperatorTok{\textless{}}\DataTypeTok{f32}\NormalTok{, [IN]}\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{tensor}\OperatorTok{\textless{}}\DataTypeTok{f32}\NormalTok{, [OUT]}\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \CommentTok{// Hidden layer}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ h }\OperatorTok{=} \FunctionTok{einsum}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"hi,i{-}\textgreater{}h"}\NormalTok{, mdl}\OperatorTok{.}\NormalTok{w1, x);}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ h\_act }\OperatorTok{=} \FunctionTok{einsum}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"i{-}\textgreater{}i"}\NormalTok{, h);}
    \CommentTok{// Output layer}
    \FunctionTok{einsum}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"oh,h{-}\textgreater{}o"}\NormalTok{, mdl}\OperatorTok{.}\NormalTok{w2, h\_act)}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{quote}
\textbf{Note:} In practice, training a neural network in IRIS involves
loading data, computing losses, and applying gradient updates. The
\texttt{einsum} operations form the computational graph; gradient
computation can be done via the \texttt{grad} mechanism described in
chapter 10 or via numerical finite differences.
\end{quote}

\subsection{13.6 Sparse Tensors}\label{sparse-tensors}

For data with many zero values, IRIS supports sparse representations:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ process\_sparse(data: }\DataTypeTok{tensor}\OperatorTok{\textless{}}\DataTypeTok{f32}\NormalTok{, [N]}\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \KeywordTok{val} \DataTypeTok{sparse} \OperatorTok{=} \FunctionTok{sparsify}\NormalTok{(data);      }\CommentTok{// convert to sparse}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ dense }\OperatorTok{=} \FunctionTok{densify}\NormalTok{(}\DataTypeTok{sparse}\NormalTok{);      }\CommentTok{// convert back to dense}
    \DecValTok{0}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Sparse tensors save memory and speed up operations when the data is
predominantly zero (e.g., embeddings, adjacency matrices).

\subsection{13.9 Model DSL (Neural Network
Architectures)}\label{model-dsl-neural-network-architectures-1}

The declarative \textbf{Model DSL} simplifies deep learning model
definitions:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{model}\NormalTok{ MLP \{}
    \KeywordTok{input}\NormalTok{ x: }\DataTypeTok{tensor}\OperatorTok{\textless{}}\DataTypeTok{f32}\NormalTok{, [batch, }\DecValTok{784}\NormalTok{]}\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}}
    \KeywordTok{layer}\NormalTok{ h1 Linear(x, in\_features}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{784}\NormalTok{, out\_features}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{128}\NormalTok{)}
    \KeywordTok{layer}\NormalTok{ a1 ReLU(h1)}
    \KeywordTok{layer}\NormalTok{ h2 Linear(a1, in\_features}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{128}\NormalTok{, out\_features}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{10}\NormalTok{)}
    \KeywordTok{output}\NormalTok{ h2}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

This model is compiled to highly efficient vector/SIMD instructions and
exposes standard weights and biases for training.

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{13.10 Machine Learning Stdlib Modules
(\texttt{std.ml}, \texttt{std.rl},
\texttt{std.nn})}{13.10 Machine Learning Stdlib Modules (std.ml, std.rl, std.nn)}}\label{machine-learning-stdlib-modules-std.ml-std.rl-std.nn-1}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{std.nn}}: Offers high-level layers (Linear, Conv2D,
  RNN), activations (Softmax, Sigmoid), loss functions (CrossEntropy,
  MSE), and optimizers (SGD, Adam).
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{std.ml}}: Traditional ML algorithms (k-Means, Gaussian
  Naive Bayes, k-NN, Linear/Logistic Regression).
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{std.rl}}: Reinforcement learning framework featuring
  FIFO replay buffers, experience sampling, stable clipped PPO
  objectives, and GAE value baselines.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Try It Yourself}\label{try-it-yourself-12}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Write a function
  \texttt{softmax(x:\ tensor\textless{}f32,\ {[}N{]}\textgreater{})\ -\textgreater{}\ tensor\textless{}f32,\ {[}N{]}\textgreater{}}
  that computes the softmax of a vector. (Hint: compute exp of each
  element, then divide by the sum.)
\item
  Write a function to compute the Frobenius norm of a matrix (square
  root of sum of squared elements) using \texttt{einsum}.
\item
  Design a three-layer MLP record type and write its \texttt{forward}
  function.
\end{enumerate}

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{13.7 Native Neural Networks
(\texttt{std.nn})}{13.7 Native Neural Networks (std.nn)}}\label{native-neural-networks-std.nn}

IRIS provides a native neural network library (\texttt{std.nn}) that
enables training models directly without Python.

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{bring std}\OperatorTok{.}\NormalTok{nn}
\CommentTok{// Create a 2{-}layer Multi{-}Layer Perceptron (MLP)}
\NormalTok{val model }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ nn}\OperatorTok{.}\NormalTok{mlp\_create([}\DecValTok{784}\OperatorTok{,} \DecValTok{128}\OperatorTok{,} \DecValTok{10}\NormalTok{])}
\CommentTok{// Train with Adam optimizer}
\NormalTok{nn}\OperatorTok{.}\NormalTok{mlp\_train\_adam(model}\OperatorTok{,}\NormalTok{ inputs}\OperatorTok{,}\NormalTok{ targets}\OperatorTok{,} \DecValTok{0.001}\OperatorTok{,} \DecValTok{10}\OperatorTok{,} \DecValTok{32}\NormalTok{)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsection{13.8 External Model Inference
(ONNX)}\label{external-model-inference-onnx}

For production deployment, you can run pre-trained ONNX models natively:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{bring std}\OperatorTok{.}\NormalTok{ml}
\NormalTok{val session }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ ml}\OperatorTok{.}\NormalTok{onnx\_load(}\StringTok{"model.onnx"}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{val result }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ ml}\OperatorTok{.}\NormalTok{onnx\_run(session}\OperatorTok{,}\NormalTok{ [input\_tensor])}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\section{Chapter 14: Native
Compilation}\label{chapter-14-native-compilation}

\subsection{14.1 Building a Native
Binary}\label{building-a-native-binary}

The \texttt{iris\ build} command compiles your IRIS source to a native
executable:

\begin{verbatim}
iris build myapp.iris -o myapp.exe
\end{verbatim}

After building, run it directly:

\begin{verbatim}
myapp.exe
\end{verbatim}

Or use \texttt{iris\ run} which compiles and runs in one step:

\begin{verbatim}
iris run myapp.iris
\end{verbatim}

\subsection{14.2 How the Compiler Pipeline
Works}\label{how-the-compiler-pipeline-works}

When you run \texttt{iris\ build}, the following steps happen:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Parse}: The \texttt{.iris} source is tokenized and parsed into
  an AST.
\item
  \textbf{Lower}: The AST is compiled to IRIS IR (a block-parameter SSA
  form similar to MLIR).
\item
  \textbf{Optimize}: Several passes run:

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    \texttt{ValidatePass} --- checks SSA invariants
  \item
    \texttt{TypeInferPass} --- ensures type consistency
  \item
    \texttt{ConstFoldPass} --- folds constant expressions
  \item
    \texttt{DcePass} --- dead code elimination
  \item
    \texttt{CsePass} --- common subexpression elimination
  \end{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{LLVM IR}: The IR is translated to LLVM IR text.
\item
  \textbf{Compile}: \texttt{clang} compiles the LLVM IR to an object
  file.
\item
  \textbf{Link}: \texttt{clang} (with \texttt{lld}) links the object
  file with the IRIS C runtime to produce the final executable.
\end{enumerate}

You can inspect the IR at each stage:

\begin{verbatim}
iris --emit ir myapp.iris        # print IRIS IR
iris --emit llvm myapp.iris      # print LLVM IR text
\end{verbatim}

\subsection{14.3 Inspecting LLVM IR}\label{inspecting-llvm-ir}

The \texttt{-\/-emit\ llvm} flag prints the LLVM IR that will be
compiled:

\begin{verbatim}
iris --emit llvm hello.iris
\end{verbatim}

This is useful for debugging performance issues or understanding what
the compiler generates.

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{14.4 Calling C Libraries with
\texttt{extern}}{14.4 Calling C Libraries with extern}}\label{calling-c-libraries-with-extern}

IRIS can call C functions using \texttt{extern\ def} declarations:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{// Declare C standard library functions}
\KeywordTok{extern} \KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ strlen(s: }\DataTypeTok{str}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}
\KeywordTok{extern} \KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ puts(s: }\DataTypeTok{str}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ main() }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ msg }\OperatorTok{=} \StringTok{"Hello from C!"}\NormalTok{;}
\NormalTok{    puts(msg);}
    \DecValTok{0}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

The \texttt{extern\ def} declaration tells IRIS the C function's name
and signature. At link time, the function must be available in a linked
library.

A more complete FFI example --- calling a C math function:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{// C\textquotesingle{}s pow function from libm}
\KeywordTok{extern} \KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ pow\_c(base: }\DataTypeTok{f64}\NormalTok{, }\FunctionTok{exp}\NormalTok{: }\DataTypeTok{f64}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{f64}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ compute\_power() }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \KeywordTok{val} \DataTypeTok{result} \OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ pow\_c(}\FloatTok{2.0} \KeywordTok{to} \DataTypeTok{f64}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{10.0} \KeywordTok{to} \DataTypeTok{f64}\NormalTok{);}
    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\DataTypeTok{result}\NormalTok{);   }\CommentTok{// 1024.0}
    \DecValTok{0}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsection{14.5 The C Runtime}\label{the-c-runtime}

IRIS programs link against a small C runtime (\texttt{iris\_runtime.c})
that provides:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Memory allocation for lists, maps, channels, and other heap objects
\item
  String operations
\item
  Channel and threading primitives (using pthreads)
\item
  Atomic operations
\item
  I/O functions
\end{itemize}

You do not need to manage memory manually --- the runtime handles
allocation and a reference-counting scheme for heap objects.

\subsection{14.6 Performance Tips}\label{performance-tips}

\textbf{Use fixed arrays for hot data paths}: \texttt{{[}T;\ N{]}}
arrays are allocated on the stack and have no overhead.
\texttt{list\textless{}T\textgreater{}} involves heap allocation.

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{// Fast: stack{-}allocated array}
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ sum\_fixed() }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ data: [}\DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{; }\DecValTok{8}\NormalTok{] }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ [}\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{3}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{4}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{5}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{6}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{7}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{8}\NormalTok{];}
    \KeywordTok{var}\NormalTok{ total }\OperatorTok{=} \DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{;}
    \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ i }\ControlFlowTok{in} \DecValTok{0}\OperatorTok{..}\DecValTok{8}\NormalTok{ \{}
\NormalTok{        total }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ total }\OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ data[i]}
\NormalTok{    \}}
\NormalTok{    total}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\textbf{Minimize allocations in hot loops}: Avoid creating new lists or
maps inside tight loops.

\textbf{Use \texttt{par\ for} for embarrassingly parallel workloads}:
When iterations are independent, \texttt{par\ for} can use multiple CPU
cores.

\textbf{Profile with timing}: Use \texttt{time\_now\_ms()} to measure
how long sections of code take:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ benchmark() }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ t0 }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ time\_now\_ms();}
    \CommentTok{// ... work ...}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ t1 }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ time\_now\_ms();}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ elapsed }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ t1 }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ t0;}
    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{f"elapsed: }\ControlFlowTok{\{}\NormalTok{elapsed}\ControlFlowTok{\}}\StringTok{ms"}\NormalTok{);}
    \DecValTok{0}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsection{Try It Yourself}\label{try-it-yourself-13}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Write a program, build it as a native binary, and run it. Measure the
  time to compute the 40th Fibonacci number both recursively and
  iteratively.
\item
  Declare and call a C function from your program (for example,
  \texttt{rand()} from the C standard library to generate random
  numbers).
\item
  Use \texttt{iris\ -\/-emit\ ir} and \texttt{iris\ -\/-emit\ llvm} to
  see what code a simple function generates.
\end{enumerate}

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\section{Chapter 15: The Standard
Library}\label{chapter-15-the-standard-library}

IRIS ships with a standard library of \texttt{.iris} files that you can
bring into your programs. Use the \texttt{bring} statement at the top of
your file:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{bring}\NormalTok{ std}\OperatorTok{.}\NormalTok{math}
\KeywordTok{bring}\NormalTok{ std}\OperatorTok{.}\NormalTok{string}
\KeywordTok{bring}\NormalTok{ std}\OperatorTok{.}\NormalTok{fmt}
\KeywordTok{bring}\NormalTok{ std}\OperatorTok{.}\NormalTok{fs}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{15.1 \texttt{std.math} --- Extended Math
Functions}{15.1 std.math --- Extended Math Functions}}\label{std.math-extended-math-functions}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{bring}\NormalTok{ std}\OperatorTok{.}\NormalTok{math}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ main() }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(gcd(}\DecValTok{48}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{18}\NormalTok{));           }\CommentTok{// 6}
    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(lcm(}\DecValTok{4}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{6}\NormalTok{));             }\CommentTok{// 12}
    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(abs\_i64(}\OperatorTok{{-}}\DecValTok{42}\NormalTok{));          }\CommentTok{// 42}
    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(is\_even(}\DecValTok{7}\NormalTok{));            }\CommentTok{// false}
    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(is\_odd(}\DecValTok{7}\NormalTok{));             }\CommentTok{// true}
    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(clamp\_i64(}\DecValTok{150}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{100}\NormalTok{)); }\CommentTok{// 100}
    \DecValTok{0}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Available functions from \texttt{std.math}:

\begin{longtable}[]{@{}ll@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
Function & Description \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
\texttt{gcd(a,\ b)} & Greatest common divisor \\
\texttt{lcm(a,\ b)} & Least common multiple \\
\texttt{abs\_i64(n)} & Absolute value for integers \\
\texttt{clamp\_i64(x,\ lo,\ hi)} & Clamp integer to range \\
\texttt{min\_i64(a,\ b)} & Integer minimum \\
\texttt{max\_i64(a,\ b)} & Integer maximum \\
\texttt{sign\_i64(n)} & Sign function: -1, 0, or 1 \\
\texttt{is\_even(n)} & True if n is divisible by 2 \\
\texttt{is\_odd(n)} & True if n is not divisible by 2 \\
\end{longtable}

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{15.2 \texttt{std.string} --- String
Utilities}{15.2 std.string --- String Utilities}}\label{std.string-string-utilities}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{bring}\NormalTok{ std}\OperatorTok{.}\NormalTok{string}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ main() }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ padded }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ pad\_left(}\StringTok{"42"}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{6}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{"0"}\NormalTok{);    }\CommentTok{// "000042"}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ trimmed }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ trim\_start(}\StringTok{"  hello  "}\NormalTok{);   }\CommentTok{// "hello  " (only left)}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ ws }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ words(}\StringTok{"hello world foo"}\NormalTok{);       }\CommentTok{// list\textless{}str\textgreater{}}

    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(padded);}
    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{len}\NormalTok{(ws));   }\CommentTok{// 3}

    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ joined }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ str\_join(ws, }\StringTok{"{-}"}\NormalTok{);}
    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(joined);    }\CommentTok{// "hello{-}world{-}foo"}
    \DecValTok{0}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Available functions from \texttt{std.string}:

\begin{longtable}[]{@{}ll@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
Function & Description \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
\texttt{trim\_start(s)} & Trim leading whitespace \\
\texttt{trim\_end(s)} & Trim trailing whitespace \\
\texttt{pad\_left(s,\ width,\ ch)} & Left-pad to width with character \\
\texttt{pad\_right(s,\ width,\ ch)} & Right-pad to width with
character \\
\texttt{words(s)} & Split on spaces, returns
\texttt{list\textless{}str\textgreater{}} \\
\texttt{lines(s)} & Split on \texttt{\textbackslash{}n}, returns
\texttt{list\textless{}str\textgreater{}} \\
\texttt{str\_join(parts,\ delim)} & Join list with delimiter \\
\texttt{is\_empty(s)} & True if \texttt{len(s)\ ==\ 0} \\
\texttt{str\_repeat(s,\ n)} & Repeat string \texttt{n} times \\
\end{longtable}

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{15.3 \texttt{std.fmt} ---
Formatting}{15.3 std.fmt --- Formatting}}\label{std.fmt-formatting}

The \texttt{fmt} module provides printf-style string formatting:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{bring}\NormalTok{ std}\OperatorTok{.}\NormalTok{fmt}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ main() }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \CommentTok{// sprintf takes a format string and list\textless{}str\textgreater{} of pre{-}stringified args}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ args }\OperatorTok{=} \DataTypeTok{list}\NormalTok{();}
    \FunctionTok{push}\NormalTok{(args, }\FunctionTok{to\_str}\NormalTok{(}\DecValTok{42}\NormalTok{));}
    \FunctionTok{push}\NormalTok{(args, }\FunctionTok{to\_str}\NormalTok{(}\FloatTok{3.14159}\NormalTok{));}

    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ s }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ sprintf(}\StringTok{"\%05d \%.2f"}\NormalTok{, args);}
    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(s);   }\CommentTok{// "00042 3.14"}

    \CommentTok{// Pad integers for table output}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ n }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ pad\_int(}\DecValTok{7}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{4}\NormalTok{);}
    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(n);   }\CommentTok{// "   7"}

    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ z }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ zero\_pad\_int(}\DecValTok{42}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{6}\NormalTok{);}
    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(z);   }\CommentTok{// "000042"}
    \DecValTok{0}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Available functions from \texttt{std.fmt}:

\begin{longtable}[]{@{}ll@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
Function & Description \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
\texttt{sprintf(fmt,\ args)} & Printf-style format string \\
\texttt{pad\_int(n,\ width)} & Right-align integer in field \\
\texttt{zero\_pad\_int(n,\ width)} & Zero-pad integer \\
\texttt{left\_align(s,\ width)} & Left-align string in field \\
\texttt{right\_align(s,\ width)} & Right-align string in field \\
\end{longtable}

Format specifiers: \texttt{\%d}, \texttt{\%s}, \texttt{\%f},
\texttt{\%g}, \texttt{\%x}, \texttt{\%i}, \texttt{\%\%} (literal
\texttt{\%}), with optional width (\texttt{\%5d}), zero-padding
(\texttt{\%05d}), left-align (\texttt{\%-8s}), and precision
(\texttt{\%.3f}).

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{15.4 \texttt{std.fs} --- File
System}{15.4 std.fs --- File System}}\label{std.fs-file-system}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{bring}\NormalTok{ std}\OperatorTok{.}\NormalTok{fs}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ main() }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \CommentTok{// Write a file}
    \KeywordTok{val} \FunctionTok{ok} \OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ write\_text(}\StringTok{"output.txt"}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{"Hello, IRIS!\textbackslash{}n"}\NormalTok{);}
    \ControlFlowTok{if} \FunctionTok{ok}\NormalTok{ \{}
        \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"wrote file"}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    \} }\ControlFlowTok{else}\NormalTok{ \{}
        \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"failed to write"}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    \};}

    \CommentTok{// Read a file}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ content }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ read\_text(}\StringTok{"output.txt"}\NormalTok{);}
    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(content);}

    \CommentTok{// Check existence}
    \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ path\_exists(}\StringTok{"output.txt"}\NormalTok{) \{}
        \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"file exists"}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    \} }\ControlFlowTok{else}\NormalTok{ \{}
        \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"no file"}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    \};}

    \CommentTok{// Read lines}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ lns }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ read\_lines(}\StringTok{"output.txt"}\NormalTok{);}
    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{list\_len}\NormalTok{(lns));}
    \DecValTok{0}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Available functions from \texttt{std.fs}:

\begin{longtable}[]{@{}ll@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
Function & Description \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
\texttt{read\_text(path)} & Read file as string (empty on error) \\
\texttt{write\_text(path,\ content)} & Write string to file, returns
\texttt{bool} \\
\texttt{path\_exists(path)} & Check if file or directory exists \\
\texttt{read\_lines(path)} & Read file as
\texttt{list\textless{}str\textgreater{}} of lines \\
\end{longtable}

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{15.5 \texttt{std.json} ---
JSON}{15.5 std.json --- JSON}}\label{std.json-json}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{bring}\NormalTok{ std}\OperatorTok{.}\NormalTok{json}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ main() }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ obj }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ json\_object();}
\NormalTok{    json\_set(obj, }\StringTok{"name"}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{"IRIS"}\NormalTok{);}
\NormalTok{    json\_set(obj, }\StringTok{"version"}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{"1.0.0{-}rc1"}\NormalTok{);}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ s }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ json\_emit(obj);}
    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(s);   }\CommentTok{// \{"name": "IRIS", "version": "1.0.0{-}rc1"\}}
    \DecValTok{0}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{15.6 \texttt{std.csv} ---
CSV}{15.6 std.csv --- CSV}}\label{std.csv-csv}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{bring}\NormalTok{ std}\OperatorTok{.}\NormalTok{csv}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ main() }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ row }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ csv\_parse\_row(}\StringTok{"Alice,30,Engineer"}\NormalTok{);}
    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{list\_get}\NormalTok{(row, }\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{));   }\CommentTok{// Alice}

    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ out }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ csv\_emit\_row(row);}
    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(out);   }\CommentTok{// Alice,30,Engineer}
    \DecValTok{0}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{15.7 \texttt{std.crypto} --- Cryptography \&
Hashing}{15.7 std.crypto --- Cryptography \& Hashing}}\label{std.crypto-cryptography-hashing}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{bring}\NormalTok{ std}\OperatorTok{.}\NormalTok{crypto}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ main() }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ h }\OperatorTok{=} \FunctionTok{sha256}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"hello"}\NormalTok{);}
    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(h);                    }\CommentTok{// hex{-}encoded SHA{-}256}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ id }\OperatorTok{=} \FunctionTok{uuid}\NormalTok{();}
    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(id);                   }\CommentTok{// random UUID v4}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ enc }\OperatorTok{=} \FunctionTok{hex\_encode}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"hi"}\NormalTok{);}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ dec }\OperatorTok{=} \FunctionTok{hex\_decode}\NormalTok{(enc);}
    \DecValTok{0}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{15.8 \texttt{std.ffi} --- Foreign Function
Interface (C, Python,
Rust)}{15.8 std.ffi --- Foreign Function Interface (C, Python, Rust)}}\label{std.ffi-foreign-function-interface-c-python-rust}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{bring}\NormalTok{ std}\OperatorTok{.}\NormalTok{ffi}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ main() }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \CommentTok{// C FFI — load shared library and call typed functions}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ lib }\OperatorTok{=} \FunctionTok{ffi\_open}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"libm.so"}\NormalTok{);}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ sq }\OperatorTok{=} \FunctionTok{ffi\_call\_f64}\NormalTok{(lib, }\StringTok{"sqrt"}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{144.0}\NormalTok{);}
    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{to\_str}\NormalTok{(sq));    }\CommentTok{// 12.0}

    \CommentTok{// Python FFI — evaluate Python expressions}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ py }\OperatorTok{=} \FunctionTok{python\_eval}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"2 ** 10"}\NormalTok{);}
    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(py);            }\CommentTok{// 1024}

    \CommentTok{// Rust cdylib FFI}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ rlib }\OperatorTok{=} \FunctionTok{rust\_lib\_open}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"mylib.dll"}\NormalTok{);}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ n }\OperatorTok{=} \FunctionTok{rust\_call\_i64}\NormalTok{(rlib, }\StringTok{"compute"}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{42}\NormalTok{);}
    \DecValTok{0}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

System}{15.9 std.os --- Operating System}}\label{std.os-operating-system}

    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(home);}

\NormalTok{\}}
