\subsection{15.12 Remaining Standard Library
Modules}\label{remaining-standard-library-modules}

IRIS ships with 25 stdlib modules total. Additional modules include:

\begin{longtable}[]{@{}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 2\tabcolsep) * \real{0.3810}}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 2\tabcolsep) * \real{0.6190}}@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
\begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Module
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Description
\end{minipage} \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
\texttt{std.http} & HTTP client (\texttt{http\_get},
\texttt{http\_post}) \\
\texttt{std.time} & Time and duration (\texttt{now}, \texttt{sleep},
\texttt{elapsed}) \\
\texttt{std.iter} & Functional iterators (\texttt{map\_list},
\texttt{filter\_list}, \texttt{reduce\_list}) \\
\texttt{std.set} & Set operations (union, intersection, difference) \\
\texttt{std.queue} & FIFO queue \\
\texttt{std.heap} & Priority queue / min-heap \\
\texttt{std.deque} & Double-ended queue \\
\texttt{std.kv} & Key-value store (SQLite-backed) \\
\texttt{std.table} & Tabular data operations \\
\texttt{std.dataset} & ML dataset abstraction \\
\texttt{std.dataframe} & DataFrame-like API \\
\texttt{std.path} & Path manipulation \\
\texttt{std.async} & Async runtime helpers \\
\texttt{std.bitset} & Bit array operations \\
\end{longtable}

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{15.13 \texttt{std.svg} \&
\texttt{std.termplot} ---
Visualizations}{15.13 std.svg \& std.termplot --- Visualizations}}\label{std.svg-std.termplot-visualizations}

The \texttt{std.svg} module provides a simple, structured API to
generate vector graphics:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{bring}\NormalTok{ std}\OperatorTok{.}\NormalTok{svg}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ generate\_chart() }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ canvas }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ svg}\OperatorTok{.}\NormalTok{canvas(}\DecValTok{800}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{600}\NormalTok{);}
\NormalTok{    svg}\OperatorTok{.}\NormalTok{rect(canvas, }\DecValTok{50}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{50}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{700}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{500}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{"fill: white; stroke: black; stroke{-}width: 2"}\NormalTok{);}
\NormalTok{    svg}\OperatorTok{.}\NormalTok{circle(canvas, }\DecValTok{400}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{300}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{150}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{"fill: blue; opacity: 0.5"}\NormalTok{);}
\NormalTok{    svg}\OperatorTok{.}\NormalTok{text(canvas, }\DecValTok{400}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{80}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{"IRIS Standard Visualization"}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{"font{-}size: 24px; text{-}anchor: middle"}\NormalTok{);}
\NormalTok{    svg}\OperatorTok{.}\NormalTok{save(canvas, }\StringTok{"chart.svg"}\NormalTok{);}
    \DecValTok{0}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

The \texttt{std.termplot} module provides instant inline charts directly
in your terminal output:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{bring}\NormalTok{ std}\OperatorTok{.}\NormalTok{termplot}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ plot\_live() }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ data }\OperatorTok{=} \DataTypeTok{list}\NormalTok{();}
    \FunctionTok{push}\NormalTok{(data, }\FloatTok{1.2}\NormalTok{); }\FunctionTok{push}\NormalTok{(data, }\FloatTok{2.5}\NormalTok{); }\FunctionTok{push}\NormalTok{(data, }\FloatTok{3.1}\NormalTok{);}
    \FunctionTok{push}\NormalTok{(data, }\FloatTok{2.0}\NormalTok{); }\FunctionTok{push}\NormalTok{(data, }\FloatTok{4.5}\NormalTok{); }\FunctionTok{push}\NormalTok{(data, }\FloatTok{5.0}\NormalTok{);}
    
    \CommentTok{// Plots a clean Unicode line chart}
\NormalTok{    termplot}\OperatorTok{.}\NormalTok{line(data, }\StringTok{"Performance Profile"}\NormalTok{);}
    \DecValTok{0}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{15.14 Using \texttt{bring} in the
REPL}{15.14 Using bring in the REPL}}\label{using-bring-in-the-repl}

In the REPL, use \texttt{:bring} to load a stdlib module:

\begin{verbatim}
>> :bring std.math
loaded: std.math
>> gcd(48, 18)
6
\end{verbatim}

\subsection{15.7 Subcommands \& Tooling
Suite}\label{subcommands-tooling-suite}

IRIS ships with a comprehensive set of developer utilities built
directly into the main \texttt{iris} compiler binary:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{iris\ test}}: Automated test discovery and execution.
  Scans code for \texttt{@test} decorators or \texttt{test\_} prefixed
  functions. Support test filtering:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\ExtensionTok{iris}\NormalTok{ test }\AttributeTok{{-}{-}filter}\NormalTok{ math}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{iris\ bench}}: Benchmarking harness. Executes
  functions tagged with \texttt{@bench} multiple times to measure
  average runtime and memory allocation.
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{iris\ profile}}: Runs a program and generates a
  performance flame graph:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\ExtensionTok{iris}\NormalTok{ profile main.iris}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{iris\ explain}}: Interactive error and diagnostic
  catalog. Explains compilation and runtime diagnostic codes with common
  causes and fixes:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\ExtensionTok{iris}\NormalTok{ explain E4}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{iris\ fmt}}: Self-contained code formatter. Rewrites
  \texttt{.iris} files to standard, idiomatic layouts.
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{iris\ lint}}: Linter that analyzes code structures for
  performance and naming style issues.
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{iris\ doc}}: Automatically extracts doc comments and
  generates Markdown/HTML API documentation.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Try It Yourself}\label{try-it-yourself-14}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Use \texttt{std.fmt} to format a table of numbers with aligned
  columns.
\item
  Use \texttt{std.fs} to write a program that reads a text file, counts
  its words, and reports the result.
\item
  Use \texttt{std.string} to write a function that normalizes a string:
  trim whitespace, convert to lowercase, and replace multiple spaces
  with a single space.
\item
  Use \texttt{std.crypto} to compute the SHA-256 hash of a file's
  contents.
\item
  Use \texttt{std.ffi} to call a C math function from IRIS.
\end{enumerate}

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\section{Chapter 16: Tooling}\label{chapter-16-tooling}

\subsection{16.1 The REPL in Depth}\label{the-repl-in-depth}

The IRIS REPL is a persistent interactive session. It supports
multi-line input when you open a brace:

\begin{verbatim}
>> def greet(name: str) -> str {
...   concat("Hello, ", name)
... }
>> greet("World")
Hello, World
\end{verbatim}

The REPL maintains state across inputs --- definitions and bindings
persist:

\begin{verbatim}
>> val x = 42
>> val y = 100
>> x + y
142
\end{verbatim}

\textbf{REPL commands:}

Every command accepts a short alias shown in parentheses.

\begin{longtable}[]{@{}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 4\tabcolsep) * \real{0.3103}}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 4\tabcolsep) * \real{0.2414}}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 4\tabcolsep) * \real{0.4483}}@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
\begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Command
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Alias
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Description
\end{minipage} \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
\texttt{:help} & \texttt{:h} & Show the full command reference \\
\texttt{:env} & \texttt{:e} & List all active definitions and
bindings \\
\texttt{:type\ \textless{}expr\textgreater{}} &
\texttt{:t\ \textless{}expr\textgreater{}} & Show the inferred type of
an expression \\
\texttt{:bring\ \textless{}mod\textgreater{}} &
\texttt{:b\ \textless{}mod\textgreater{}} & Load a stdlib module
(e.g.~\texttt{:bring\ std.math}) \\
\texttt{:time} & & Show elapsed wall-clock time of the last
evaluation \\
\texttt{:history} & & Show numbered input history for this session \\
\texttt{:clear} & & Clear the terminal screen \\
\texttt{:ir\ \textless{}expr\textgreater{}} & & Show the compiled IRIS
IR for an expression \\
\texttt{:reset} & & Clear all session state and start fresh \\
\texttt{:quit} & \texttt{:q} & Exit the REPL (also Ctrl+D or Ctrl+C) \\
\end{longtable}

\textbf{Commands in detail:}

\texttt{:help} / \texttt{:h} --- Print the table of all available
commands and their aliases.

\texttt{:env} / \texttt{:e} --- List all active definitions and bindings
in the current session:

\begin{verbatim}
>> def square(x: i64) -> i64 { x * x }
>> val n = 7
>> :env
  Definitions:
    def square(x: i64) -> i64 { x * x }
  Bindings:
    val n: i64 = 7
\end{verbatim}

\texttt{:type\ \textless{}expr\textgreater{}} /
\texttt{:t\ \textless{}expr\textgreater{}} --- Discover the type of an
expression without evaluating it:

\begin{verbatim}
>> :type 3 + 4
: i64
>> :type "hello"
: str
>> :type 3.14
: f32
>> :t true
: bool
\end{verbatim}

\texttt{:bring\ \textless{}mod\textgreater{}} /
\texttt{:b\ \textless{}mod\textgreater{}} --- Load a stdlib module into
the current session:

\begin{verbatim}
>> :bring std.math
loaded: std.math
>> gcd(12, 8)
4
\end{verbatim}

\texttt{:time} --- Show how long the last evaluation took:

\begin{verbatim}
>> val fib = 100000
>> :time
last evaluation took 0.124ms
\end{verbatim}

\texttt{:history} --- Show every input entered so far this session,
numbered:

\begin{verbatim}
>> :history
  [1] val x = 42
  [2] val y = 100
  [3] x + y
\end{verbatim}

\texttt{:clear} --- Clear the terminal screen (sends ANSI escape codes).

\texttt{:ir\ \textless{}expr\textgreater{}} --- Compile an expression
and show the resulting IRIS IR:

\begin{verbatim}
>> :ir 2 + 3
function __eval_0() -> i64 {
  block0:
    %0 = const 2 : i64
    %1 = const 3 : i64
    %2 = add %0, %1 : i64
    return %2
}
\end{verbatim}

\texttt{:reset} --- Clear all session state and start fresh:

\begin{verbatim}
>> :reset
session cleared
\end{verbatim}

\texttt{:quit} / \texttt{:q} --- Exit the REPL (also Ctrl+D or Ctrl+C).

\subsection{16.2 LSP Features}\label{lsp-features}

The IRIS Language Server Protocol implementation provides a rich editing
experience in any LSP-compatible editor. Start the server with:

\begin{verbatim}
iris lsp
\end{verbatim}

The server communicates over stdin/stdout using JSON-RPC (Language
Server Protocol v3.17).

\subsubsection{Core Features}\label{core-features}

\textbf{Hover documentation}: Hover over a function call to see its
signature and type information.

\textbf{Error diagnostics}: Errors appear as red/yellow underlines as
you type. Each diagnostic carries a machine-readable code
(e.g.~\texttt{E0001}, \texttt{E0100}) for easy lookup. Hover to see the
full error message with suggestions.

\textbf{Go to definition}: Ctrl+Click (or F12) on a function name to
jump to where it is defined.

\textbf{Auto-completion}: Press Ctrl+Space to see completions for
function names, field names, keywords, and bring-accessible stdlib
symbols.

\textbf{Outline view}: The sidebar shows all functions and definitions
in the current file.

\textbf{Signature help}: When you type a \texttt{(} after a function
name, the parameter list and expected types appear.

\textbf{Document formatting}: Run ``Format Document'' to auto-format the
current file.

\subsubsection{Code Actions (Quick
Fixes)}\label{code-actions-quick-fixes}

When the editor underlines an error, a lightbulb icon appears with
one-click fixes:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Add missing \texttt{bring}}: If you call \texttt{gcd(12,\ 8)}
  without importing \texttt{std.math}, the code action inserts
  \texttt{bring\ std.math} at the top of the file.
\item
  \textbf{Prefix unused variable}: If a variable is declared but never
  used, the code action renames it with an \texttt{\_} prefix to
  suppress the warning.
\item
  \textbf{Insert closing brace}: If a block is left unterminated, the
  code action inserts the missing \texttt{\}}.
\item
  \textbf{Extract to variable}: Select an expression and extract it into
  a \texttt{val} binding.
\end{itemize}

\subsubsection{Inlay Hints}\label{inlay-hints}

The LSP server can display inline type annotations next to \texttt{val}
and \texttt{var} bindings that omit explicit types:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ example() }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ x }\OperatorTok{=} \DecValTok{42}\NormalTok{;          }\CommentTok{// inlay hint: \textasciigrave{}: i64\textasciigrave{}}
    \KeywordTok{var}\NormalTok{ name }\OperatorTok{=} \StringTok{"IRIS"}\NormalTok{;   }\CommentTok{// inlay hint: \textasciigrave{}: str\textasciigrave{}}
    \DecValTok{0}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Enable or disable this in your editor's settings.

\subsubsection{Find All References}\label{find-all-references}

Right-click an identifier and choose ``Find All References'' (or
Shift+F12) to see every location in the current file where that name is
used --- definitions, calls, and assignments.

\subsubsection{Rename Symbol}\label{rename-symbol}

Press F2 on a function or variable name to rename it everywhere it
appears. The LSP server computes all occurrences and applies the rename
atomically.

\subsubsection{Diagnostic Codes}\label{diagnostic-codes}

Every error and warning carries a diagnostic code for quick reference:

\begin{longtable}[]{@{}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 2\tabcolsep) * \real{0.5455}}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 2\tabcolsep) * \real{0.4545}}@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
\begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Code Range
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Category
\end{minipage} \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
\texttt{E0001} -- \texttt{E0006} & Parse errors (unexpected character,
unterminated string, invalid literal, etc.) \\
\texttt{E0100} -- \texttt{E0107} & Lowering errors (undefined variable,
type mismatch, duplicate function, etc.) \\
\texttt{E0200} -- \texttt{E0205} & Pass errors (use-before-def, multiple
definition, type error, shape mismatch, etc.) \\
\texttt{E0300} & Code generation errors \\
\texttt{E0400} & Interpreter errors \\
\texttt{E0500} & I/O errors \\
\end{longtable}

See \hyperref[appendix-e-compiler-error-reference]{Appendix E} for
detailed descriptions and fixes.

\subsection{16.3 The Step Debugger (DAP)}\label{the-step-debugger-dap}

IRIS implements the Debug Adapter Protocol (DAP), which integrates with
VS Code's debugging panel and other compatible debuggers.

Start the debug adapter:

\begin{verbatim}
iris dap
\end{verbatim}

From VS Code with the IRIS extension, press F5 to start a debugging
session.

\subsubsection{Core Debugging}\label{core-debugging}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Breakpoints}: Click in the gutter to set a breakpoint on a
  line.
\item
  \textbf{Step over (F10)}: Execute the current line and move to the
  next.
\item
  \textbf{Step into (F11)}: Step into a function call.
\item
  \textbf{Step out (Shift+F11)}: Run until the current function returns.
\item
  \textbf{Continue (F5)}: Resume execution until the next breakpoint.
\item
  \textbf{Variables panel}: See all local variables and their current
  values.
\end{itemize}

\subsubsection{Advanced Features}\label{advanced-features}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Step back}: Reverse one step to the previous statement. Useful
  for inspecting a value you just passed --- press the step-back button
  in VS Code's debug toolbar or use the \texttt{stepBack} command.
\item
  \textbf{Hover evaluation}: Hover over any variable or expression in
  the source while paused to see its current value in a tooltip. The
  debugger evaluates the expression in the current scope context.
\item
  \textbf{Debug Console evaluation}: Type arbitrary IRIS expressions in
  the Debug Console to evaluate them in the current scope. Supports
  arithmetic, variable lookup, and simple function calls.
\item
  \textbf{Call stack}: The Call Stack panel shows the full chain of
  function calls leading to the current position, with source locations
  for each frame.
\item
  \textbf{Loaded sources}: View which source files the debugger has
  loaded via the ``Loaded Sources'' panel.
\item
  \textbf{Exception info}: When a runtime error occurs, the debugger
  reports exception details including the error description and break
  mode so you can inspect the program state at the point of failure.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{16.4 The VS Code Extension}\label{the-vs-code-extension}

The official IRIS VS Code extension (\texttt{iris-lang}) bundles the LSP
client, DAP client, and additional editor features.

\subsubsection{Installation}\label{installation-1}

\begin{verbatim}
code --install-extension iris-lang-1.0.0-rc1.vsix
\end{verbatim}

Or install from the Extensions panel in VS Code by searching for ``IRIS
Language''.

\subsubsection{Features}\label{features}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Syntax highlighting}: Full TextMate grammar for \texttt{.iris}
  files --- keywords, types, strings, comments, numbers, and operators.
\item
  \textbf{Error diagnostics}: Real-time error and warning underlines
  powered by the LSP server.
\item
  \textbf{Code actions}: Lightbulb quick fixes appear automatically for
  common errors.
\item
  \textbf{Inlay hints}: Inline type annotations for
  \texttt{val}/\texttt{var} bindings.
\item
  \textbf{Go to definition, Find References, Rename}: Standard IDE
  navigation.
\item
  \textbf{Debugging}: Press F5 to launch a debug session with full
  breakpoint, step, and variable inspection support.
\item
  \textbf{Server status}: The status bar shows the IRIS language server
  state. Click to see options:

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    \emph{Restart Server} --- restart the LSP server without reloading
    the window.
  \item
    \emph{Stop Server} --- stop the language server.
  \item
    \emph{Show Output} --- view the server's log output channel.
  \end{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Execution timing}: After running or building an IRIS file, the
  output channel shows the elapsed time.
\end{itemize}

\subsubsection{Extension Settings}\label{extension-settings}

\begin{longtable}[]{@{}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 4\tabcolsep) * \real{0.2903}}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 4\tabcolsep) * \real{0.2903}}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 4\tabcolsep) * \real{0.4194}}@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
\begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Setting
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Default
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Description
\end{minipage} \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
\texttt{iris.compilerPath} & \texttt{iris} & Path to the \texttt{iris}
executable \\
\texttt{iris.enableInlayHints} & \texttt{true} & Show inline type
annotations \\
\texttt{iris.maxDiagnostics} & \texttt{100} & Maximum number of
diagnostics per file \\
\end{longtable}

\subsection{16.6 IR Inspection}\label{ir-inspection}

The \texttt{-\/-emit} flag controls what the compiler outputs instead of
running the program:

\begin{verbatim}
iris --emit ir file.iris       # IRIS IR (human-readable SSA form)
iris --emit llvm file.iris     # LLVM IR text (.ll format)
\end{verbatim}

Example IR output for a simple addition function:

\begin{verbatim}
// IRIS IR for: def add(a: i64, b: i64) -> i64 { a + b }

function add(a: i64, b: i64) -> i64 {
  block0:
    %2 = add %0, %1 : i64
    return %2
}
\end{verbatim}

This is useful for:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Understanding how the optimizer transforms your code
\item
  Debugging unexpected behavior
\item
  Learning how the compiler works
\end{itemize}

\subsection{16.5 Optimization Passes}\label{optimization-passes}

The compiler runs a pipeline of optimization passes. You can see the IR
after each pass with \texttt{-\/-dump-ir-after}:

\begin{verbatim}
iris --emit ir --dump-ir-after const_fold file.iris
\end{verbatim}

Pass pipeline:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{ValidatePass} --- SSA structural validation (catches malformed
  IR)
\item
  \textbf{TypeInferPass} --- Type consistency (checks binary operand
  types match)
\item
  \textbf{ConstFoldPass} --- Constant folding (e.g., \texttt{2\ +\ 3} →
  \texttt{5} at compile time)
\item
  \textbf{OpExpandPass} --- Expands activation calls to tensor
  operations
\item
  \textbf{DcePass} --- Dead code elimination (removes unused
  computations)
\item
  \textbf{CsePass} --- Common subexpression elimination (deduplicates
  repeated computations)
\item
  \textbf{ShapeCheckPass} --- Tensor shape consistency and einsum
  notation validation
\end{enumerate}

\subsection{16.7 Subcommands \& Tooling
Suite}\label{subcommands-tooling-suite-1}

IRIS ships with a comprehensive set of developer utilities built
directly into the main \texttt{iris} compiler binary:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{iris\ test}}: Automated test discovery and execution.
  Scans code for \texttt{@test} decorators or \texttt{test\_} prefixed
  functions. Support test filtering:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\ExtensionTok{iris}\NormalTok{ test }\AttributeTok{{-}{-}filter}\NormalTok{ math}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{iris\ bench}}: Benchmarking harness. Executes
  functions tagged with \texttt{@bench} multiple times to measure
  average runtime and memory allocation.
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{iris\ profile}}: Runs a program and generates a
  performance flame graph:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\ExtensionTok{iris}\NormalTok{ profile main.iris}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{iris\ explain}}: Interactive error and diagnostic
  catalog. Explains compilation and runtime diagnostic codes with common
  causes and fixes:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\ExtensionTok{iris}\NormalTok{ explain E4}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{iris\ fmt}}: Self-contained code formatter. Rewrites
  \texttt{.iris} files to standard, idiomatic layouts.
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{iris\ lint}}: Linter that analyzes code structures for
  performance and naming style issues.
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{iris\ doc}}: Automatically extracts doc comments and
  generates Markdown/HTML API documentation.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Try It Yourself}\label{try-it-yourself-15}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Open the REPL and experiment with \texttt{:type} to learn what type
  various expressions have.
\item
  Write a function with a bug, then use the DAP debugger to step through
  and find it.
\item
  Use \texttt{iris\ -\/-emit\ ir} on a function with constant
  expressions and observe how the \texttt{ConstFoldPass} eliminates
  them.
\end{enumerate}

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\section{Chapter 17: Package Manager}\label{chapter-17-package-manager}

IRIS includes a production-grade package manager and build tool built
directly into the CLI as the \texttt{iris\ pkg} subcommand.

\subsection{17.1 Initializing a Project}\label{initializing-a-project}

Create a new structured IRIS package with:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\ExtensionTok{iris}\NormalTok{ pkg init my\_project}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

This creates the standard project layout:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{my\_project/}
\NormalTok{├── iris.toml     \# Manifest file}
\NormalTok{├── iris.lock     \# Lockfile (generated on build)}
\NormalTok{└── src/}
\NormalTok{    └── main.iris \# Entry point}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{17.2 The \texttt{iris.toml}
Manifest}{17.2 The iris.toml Manifest}}\label{the-iris.toml-manifest}

The manifest defines package metadata and third-party dependencies:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{[package]}
\DataTypeTok{name} \OperatorTok{=} \StringTok{"my\_project"}
\DataTypeTok{version} \OperatorTok{=} \StringTok{"1.0.0{-}rc1"}
\DataTypeTok{authors} \OperatorTok{=} \OperatorTok{[}\StringTok{"Moon9t"}\OperatorTok{]}

\KeywordTok{[dependencies]}
\DataTypeTok{http\_utils} \OperatorTok{=} \OperatorTok{\{ }\DataTypeTok{git}\OperatorTok{ =} \StringTok{"https://github.com/iris{-}lang/http\_utils.git"}\OperatorTok{, }\DataTypeTok{tag}\OperatorTok{ =} \StringTok{"v1.2.0"}\OperatorTok{ \}}
\DataTypeTok{json\_helper} \OperatorTok{=} \OperatorTok{\{ }\DataTypeTok{path}\OperatorTok{ =} \StringTok{"../json\_helper"}\OperatorTok{ \}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsection{17.3 Managing Dependencies}\label{managing-dependencies}

Add dependencies easily using the CLI:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\ExtensionTok{iris}\NormalTok{ pkg add http\_utils }\AttributeTok{{-}{-}git}\NormalTok{ https://github.com/iris{-}lang/http\_utils.git}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

This automatically downloads, validates, and adds the dependency to your
\texttt{iris.toml}.

\subsection{17.4 Package Subcommands}\label{package-subcommands}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{iris\ pkg\ build}}: Resolves dependencies, compiles
  them, and builds the current package.
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{iris\ pkg\ run}}: Compiles and runs the package entry
  point.
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{iris\ pkg\ update}}: Updates lockfile and checks for
  newer compatible dependency versions.
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{iris\ pkg\ list}}: Lists all active project
  dependencies.
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{iris\ pkg\ check}}: Rapidly parses and checks package
  types without full compilation.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Try It Yourself}\label{try-it-yourself-16}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Run \texttt{iris\ pkg\ init\ calc\_project} to initialize a new
  package.
\item
  Edit \texttt{iris.toml} to set yourself as the author.
\item
  Build and run it using \texttt{iris\ pkg\ run}.
\end{enumerate}

\section{Chapter 18: Building Real
Programs}\label{chapter-18-building-real-programs}

\subsection{18.1 Project Layout}\label{project-layout}

A typical IRIS project looks like this:

\begin{verbatim}
myproject/
  src/
    main.iris          # entry point
    utils.iris         # utility functions
    models.iris        # data model definitions
  data/
    input.txt
  out/
    myproject.exe      # compiled binary
\end{verbatim}

IRIS includes a robust built-in package manager called
\texttt{iris\ pkg}. You build from the entry point:

\begin{verbatim}
iris build src/main.iris -o out/myproject.exe
\end{verbatim}

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{18.2 Multi-File Programs with
\texttt{bring}}{18.2 Multi-File Programs with bring}}\label{multi-file-programs-with-bring}

The \texttt{bring} statement imports another IRIS file. All
\texttt{pub\ def} functions from that file become available:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{// src/utils.iris}
\KeywordTok{pub} \KeywordTok{def} \FunctionTok{clamp}\NormalTok{(x: }\DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{, lo: }\DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{, hi: }\DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ x }\OperatorTok{\textless{}}\NormalTok{ lo \{ lo \} }\ControlFlowTok{else}\NormalTok{ \{ }\ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ x }\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}}\NormalTok{ hi \{ hi \} }\ControlFlowTok{else}\NormalTok{ \{ x \} \}}
\NormalTok{\}}

\KeywordTok{pub} \KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ square(x: }\DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
\NormalTok{    x }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ x}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{// src/main.iris}
\KeywordTok{bring}\NormalTok{ utils}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ main() }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{clamp}\NormalTok{(}\DecValTok{150}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{100}\NormalTok{));   }\CommentTok{// 100}
    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(square(}\DecValTok{7}\NormalTok{));            }\CommentTok{// 49}
    \DecValTok{0}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Only \texttt{pub\ def} functions are exported. Private helpers stay
private to their file.

\begin{quote}
\textbf{Note:} ALL helper functions in a file that you want to use from
other files must be \texttt{pub\ def}.
\end{quote}

\subsection{18.3 Writing a Command-Line
Tool}\label{writing-a-command-line-tool}

A number-guessing game as a complete command-line program:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ main() }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \CommentTok{// Simple "guess the number" game}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ secret }\OperatorTok{=} \DecValTok{42}\NormalTok{;    }\CommentTok{// in a real game, use a random number}
    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"Guess a number between 1 and 100:"}\NormalTok{);}

    \KeywordTok{var}\NormalTok{ guesses }\OperatorTok{=} \DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{;}
    \KeywordTok{var}\NormalTok{ found }\OperatorTok{=} \ConstantTok{false}\NormalTok{;}
    \ControlFlowTok{while}\NormalTok{ found }\OperatorTok{==} \ConstantTok{false}\NormalTok{ \{}
        \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ line }\OperatorTok{=} \FunctionTok{read\_line}\NormalTok{();}
        \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ parsed }\OperatorTok{=} \FunctionTok{parse\_i64}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{trim}\NormalTok{(line));}
        \ControlFlowTok{if} \FunctionTok{is\_some}\NormalTok{(parsed) \{}
            \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ guess }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ unwrap(parsed);}
\NormalTok{            guesses }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ guesses }\OperatorTok{+} \DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{;}
            \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ guess }\OperatorTok{\textless{}}\NormalTok{ secret \{}
                \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"Too low! Try again:"}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{            \} }\ControlFlowTok{else}\NormalTok{ \{}
                \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ guess }\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}}\NormalTok{ secret \{}
                    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"Too high! Try again:"}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{                \} }\ControlFlowTok{else}\NormalTok{ \{}
\NormalTok{                    found }\OperatorTok{=} \ConstantTok{true}\NormalTok{;}
                    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{f"Correct! You got it in }\ControlFlowTok{\{}\NormalTok{guesses}\ControlFlowTok{\}}\StringTok{ guesses."}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{                \}}
\NormalTok{            \}}
\NormalTok{        \} }\ControlFlowTok{else}\NormalTok{ \{}
            \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"Please enter a valid number:"}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{        \}}
\NormalTok{    \}}
    \DecValTok{0}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsection{18.4 Writing a Word-Count
Tool}\label{writing-a-word-count-tool}

A more practical command-line tool --- counting words in a file:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{bring}\NormalTok{ std}\OperatorTok{.}\NormalTok{string}
\KeywordTok{bring}\NormalTok{ std}\OperatorTok{.}\NormalTok{fs}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ count\_words\_in\_text(text: }\DataTypeTok{str}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ ws }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ words(text);}
    \FunctionTok{list\_len}\NormalTok{(ws)}
\NormalTok{\}}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ count\_lines\_in\_text(text: }\DataTypeTok{str}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ ls }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ lines(text);}
    \FunctionTok{list\_len}\NormalTok{(ls)}
\NormalTok{\}}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ main() }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ args }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ process\_args();}
    \ControlFlowTok{if} \FunctionTok{list\_len}\NormalTok{(args) }\OperatorTok{\textless{}} \DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{ \{}
        \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"Usage: wc \textless{}filename\textgreater{}"}\NormalTok{);}
        \DecValTok{1}
\NormalTok{    \} }\ControlFlowTok{else}\NormalTok{ \{}
        \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ filename }\OperatorTok{=} \FunctionTok{list\_get}\NormalTok{(args, }\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{);}
        \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ content }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ read\_text(filename);}
        \ControlFlowTok{if} \FunctionTok{len}\NormalTok{(content) }\OperatorTok{==} \DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{ \{}
            \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{f"Could not read file: }\ControlFlowTok{\{}\NormalTok{filename}\ControlFlowTok{\}}\StringTok{"}\NormalTok{);}
            \DecValTok{1}
\NormalTok{        \} }\ControlFlowTok{else}\NormalTok{ \{}
            \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ line\_count }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ count\_lines\_in\_text(content);}
            \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ word\_count }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ count\_words\_in\_text(content);}
            \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ byte\_count }\OperatorTok{=} \FunctionTok{len}\NormalTok{(content);}
            \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{f"}\ControlFlowTok{\{}\NormalTok{line\_count}\ControlFlowTok{\}}\StringTok{ lines, }\ControlFlowTok{\{}\NormalTok{word\_count}\ControlFlowTok{\}}\StringTok{ words, }\ControlFlowTok{\{}\NormalTok{byte\_count}\ControlFlowTok{\}}\StringTok{ bytes"}\NormalTok{);}
            \DecValTok{0}
\NormalTok{        \}}
\NormalTok{    \}}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Build and run:

\begin{verbatim}
iris build wc.iris -o wc.exe
wc.exe myfile.txt
\end{verbatim}

\subsection{18.5 A Simple TCP Echo
Server}\label{a-simple-tcp-echo-server}

IRIS has built-in TCP networking:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ handle\_connection(conn: }\DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \KeywordTok{var}\NormalTok{ running }\OperatorTok{=} \ConstantTok{true}\NormalTok{;}
    \ControlFlowTok{while}\NormalTok{ running \{}
        \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ line }\OperatorTok{=} \FunctionTok{tcp\_read}\NormalTok{(conn);}
        \ControlFlowTok{if} \FunctionTok{len}\NormalTok{(line) }\OperatorTok{==} \DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{ \{}
\NormalTok{            running }\OperatorTok{=} \ConstantTok{false}
\NormalTok{        \} }\ControlFlowTok{else}\NormalTok{ \{}
            \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ response }\OperatorTok{=} \FunctionTok{concat}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"echo: "}\NormalTok{, }\FunctionTok{concat}\NormalTok{(line, }\StringTok{"\textbackslash{}n"}\NormalTok{));}
            \FunctionTok{tcp\_write}\NormalTok{(conn, response)}
\NormalTok{        \}}
\NormalTok{    \}}
    \FunctionTok{tcp\_close}\NormalTok{(conn);}
    \DecValTok{0}
\NormalTok{\}}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ main() }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ port }\OperatorTok{=} \DecValTok{8080}\NormalTok{;}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ listener }\OperatorTok{=} \FunctionTok{tcp\_listen}\NormalTok{(port);}
    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{f"Listening on port }\ControlFlowTok{\{}\NormalTok{port}\ControlFlowTok{\}}\StringTok{..."}\NormalTok{);}

    \CommentTok{// Accept one connection for demonstration}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ conn }\OperatorTok{=} \FunctionTok{tcp\_accept}\NormalTok{(listener);}
    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"Connection accepted"}\NormalTok{);}
\NormalTok{    handle\_connection(conn);}
    \FunctionTok{tcp\_close}\NormalTok{(listener);}
    \DecValTok{0}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Connect to test it with \texttt{telnet\ localhost\ 8080} or
\texttt{nc\ localhost\ 8080}.

\subsection{18.6 Performance Profiling}\label{performance-profiling}

Use \texttt{time\_now\_ms()} to build simple profiling wrappers:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ main() }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \CommentTok{// Profile different implementations}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ t0 }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ time\_now\_ms();}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ r1 }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ fib\_recursive(}\DecValTok{35}\NormalTok{);}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ t1 }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ time\_now\_ms();}

    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ t2 }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ time\_now\_ms();}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ r2 }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ fib\_iter(}\DecValTok{35}\NormalTok{);}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ t3 }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ time\_now\_ms();}

    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ time\_recursive }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ t1 }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ t0;}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ time\_iterative }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ t3 }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ t2;}
    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{f"Recursive: }\ControlFlowTok{\{}\NormalTok{r1}\ControlFlowTok{\}}\StringTok{ in }\ControlFlowTok{\{}\NormalTok{time\_recursive}\ControlFlowTok{\}}\StringTok{ms"}\NormalTok{);}
    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{f"Iterative: }\ControlFlowTok{\{}\NormalTok{r2}\ControlFlowTok{\}}\StringTok{ in }\ControlFlowTok{\{}\NormalTok{time\_iterative}\ControlFlowTok{\}}\StringTok{ms"}\NormalTok{);}
    \DecValTok{0}
\NormalTok{\}}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ fib\_recursive(n: }\DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ n }\OperatorTok{\textless{}=} \DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{ \{ n \} }\ControlFlowTok{else}\NormalTok{ \{ fib\_recursive(n}\OperatorTok{{-}}\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ fib\_recursive(n}\OperatorTok{{-}}\DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{) \}}
\NormalTok{\}}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ fib\_iter(n: }\DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ n }\OperatorTok{\textless{}=} \DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{ \{}
\NormalTok{        n}
\NormalTok{    \} }\ControlFlowTok{else}\NormalTok{ \{}
        \KeywordTok{var}\NormalTok{ a }\OperatorTok{=} \DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{;}
        \KeywordTok{var}\NormalTok{ b }\OperatorTok{=} \DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{;}
        \KeywordTok{var}\NormalTok{ i }\OperatorTok{=} \DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{;}
        \ControlFlowTok{while}\NormalTok{ i }\OperatorTok{\textless{}=}\NormalTok{ n \{}
            \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ tmp }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ a }\OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ b;}
\NormalTok{            a }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ b;}
\NormalTok{            b }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ tmp;}
\NormalTok{            i }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ i }\OperatorTok{+} \DecValTok{1}
\NormalTok{        \}}
\NormalTok{        b}
\NormalTok{    \}}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsection{18.7 A Key-Value Store
Server}\label{a-key-value-store-server}

A simple in-memory key-value store served over TCP:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ parse\_command(line: }\DataTypeTok{str}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}}\NormalTok{ (}\DataTypeTok{str}\NormalTok{, }\DataTypeTok{str}\NormalTok{, }\DataTypeTok{str}\NormalTok{) \{}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ parts }\OperatorTok{=} \FunctionTok{split}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{trim}\NormalTok{(line), }\StringTok{" "}\NormalTok{);}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ n }\OperatorTok{=} \FunctionTok{list\_len}\NormalTok{(parts);}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ cmd }\OperatorTok{=} \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ n }\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}} \DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{ \{ }\FunctionTok{list\_get}\NormalTok{(parts, }\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{) \} }\ControlFlowTok{else}\NormalTok{ \{ }\StringTok{""}\NormalTok{ \};}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ key }\OperatorTok{=} \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ n }\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}} \DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{ \{ }\FunctionTok{list\_get}\NormalTok{(parts, }\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{) \} }\ControlFlowTok{else}\NormalTok{ \{ }\StringTok{""}\NormalTok{ \};}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ val\_ }\OperatorTok{=} \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ n }\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}} \DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{ \{ }\FunctionTok{list\_get}\NormalTok{(parts, }\DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{) \} }\ControlFlowTok{else}\NormalTok{ \{ }\StringTok{""}\NormalTok{ \};}
\NormalTok{    (cmd, key, val\_)}
\NormalTok{\}}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ handle\_cmd(store: }\DataTypeTok{map}\OperatorTok{\textless{}}\DataTypeTok{str}\NormalTok{, }\DataTypeTok{str}\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}}\NormalTok{, conn: }\DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{, cmd: }\DataTypeTok{str}\NormalTok{, key: }\DataTypeTok{str}\NormalTok{, value: }\DataTypeTok{str}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{bool}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \ControlFlowTok{when}\NormalTok{ cmd \{}
        \StringTok{"SET"} \OperatorTok{=\textgreater{}}\NormalTok{ \{}
            \FunctionTok{map\_set}\NormalTok{(store, key, value);}
            \FunctionTok{tcp\_write}\NormalTok{(conn, }\StringTok{"OK\textbackslash{}n"}\NormalTok{);}
            \ConstantTok{true}
\NormalTok{        \},}
        \StringTok{"GET"} \OperatorTok{=\textgreater{}}\NormalTok{ \{}
            \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ found }\OperatorTok{=} \FunctionTok{map\_get}\NormalTok{(store, key);}
            \ControlFlowTok{if} \FunctionTok{is\_some}\NormalTok{(found) \{}
                \FunctionTok{tcp\_write}\NormalTok{(conn, }\FunctionTok{concat}\NormalTok{(unwrap(found), }\StringTok{"\textbackslash{}n"}\NormalTok{))}
\NormalTok{            \} }\ControlFlowTok{else}\NormalTok{ \{}
                \FunctionTok{tcp\_write}\NormalTok{(conn, }\StringTok{"NIL\textbackslash{}n"}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{            \};}
            \ConstantTok{true}
\NormalTok{        \},}
        \StringTok{"DEL"} \OperatorTok{=\textgreater{}}\NormalTok{ \{}
\NormalTok{            map\_remove(store, key);}
            \FunctionTok{tcp\_write}\NormalTok{(conn, }\StringTok{"OK\textbackslash{}n"}\NormalTok{);}
            \ConstantTok{true}
\NormalTok{        \},}
        \StringTok{"QUIT"} \OperatorTok{=\textgreater{}}\NormalTok{ \{}
            \FunctionTok{tcp\_write}\NormalTok{(conn, }\StringTok{"BYE\textbackslash{}n"}\NormalTok{);}
            \ConstantTok{false}
\NormalTok{        \},}
\NormalTok{        \_ }\OperatorTok{=\textgreater{}}\NormalTok{ \{}
            \FunctionTok{tcp\_write}\NormalTok{(conn, }\StringTok{"ERR unknown command\textbackslash{}n"}\NormalTok{);}
            \ConstantTok{true}
\NormalTok{        \}}
\NormalTok{    \}}
\NormalTok{\}}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ main() }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ store }\OperatorTok{=} \DataTypeTok{map}\NormalTok{();}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ listener }\OperatorTok{=} \FunctionTok{tcp\_listen}\NormalTok{(}\DecValTok{7777}\NormalTok{);}
    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"KV store listening on port 7777..."}\NormalTok{);}

    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ conn }\OperatorTok{=} \FunctionTok{tcp\_accept}\NormalTok{(listener);}
    \KeywordTok{var}\NormalTok{ running }\OperatorTok{=} \ConstantTok{true}\NormalTok{;}
    \ControlFlowTok{while}\NormalTok{ running \{}
        \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ line }\OperatorTok{=} \FunctionTok{tcp\_read}\NormalTok{(conn);}
        \ControlFlowTok{if} \FunctionTok{len}\NormalTok{(line) }\OperatorTok{==} \DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{ \{}
\NormalTok{            running }\OperatorTok{=} \ConstantTok{false}
\NormalTok{        \} }\ControlFlowTok{else}\NormalTok{ \{}
            \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ cmd\_tuple }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ parse\_command(line);}
            \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ cmd }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ cmd\_tuple}\OperatorTok{.}\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{;}
            \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ key }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ cmd\_tuple}\OperatorTok{.}\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{;}
            \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ value }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ cmd\_tuple}\OperatorTok{.}\DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{;}
\NormalTok{            running }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ handle\_cmd(store, conn, cmd, key, value)}
\NormalTok{        \}}
\NormalTok{    \}}
    \FunctionTok{tcp\_close}\NormalTok{(conn);}
    \FunctionTok{tcp\_close}\NormalTok{(listener);}
    \DecValTok{0}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsection{Try It Yourself}\label{try-it-yourself-17}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Extend the word-count tool to also count unique words using a
  \texttt{map\textless{}str,\ i64\textgreater{}}.
\item
  Build a simple calculator that reads expressions like \texttt{3\ +\ 4}
  from stdin and prints the result.
\item
  Add a \texttt{KEYS} command to the KV store server that lists all
  stored keys.
\end{enumerate}

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\section{Chapter 19: Package Manager}\label{chapter-19-package-manager}

IRIS includes a production-grade package manager and build tool built
directly into the CLI as the \texttt{iris\ pkg} subcommand.

\subsection{19.1 Initializing a Project}\label{initializing-a-project-1}

Create a new structured IRIS package with:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\ExtensionTok{iris}\NormalTok{ pkg init my\_project}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

This creates the standard project layout:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{my\_project/}
\NormalTok{├── iris.toml     \# Manifest file}
\NormalTok{├── iris.lock     \# Lockfile (generated on build)}
\NormalTok{└── src/}
\NormalTok{    └── main.iris \# Entry point}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{19.2 The \texttt{iris.toml}
Manifest}{19.2 The iris.toml Manifest}}\label{the-iris.toml-manifest-1}

The manifest defines package metadata and third-party dependencies:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{[package]}
\DataTypeTok{name} \OperatorTok{=} \StringTok{"my\_project"}
\DataTypeTok{version} \OperatorTok{=} \StringTok{"1.0.0{-}rc1"}
\DataTypeTok{authors} \OperatorTok{=} \OperatorTok{[}\StringTok{"Moon9t"}\OperatorTok{]}

\KeywordTok{[dependencies]}
\DataTypeTok{http\_utils} \OperatorTok{=} \OperatorTok{\{ }\DataTypeTok{git}\OperatorTok{ =} \StringTok{"https://github.com/iris{-}lang/http\_utils.git"}\OperatorTok{, }\DataTypeTok{tag}\OperatorTok{ =} \StringTok{"v1.2.0"}\OperatorTok{ \}}
\DataTypeTok{json\_helper} \OperatorTok{=} \OperatorTok{\{ }\DataTypeTok{path}\OperatorTok{ =} \StringTok{"../json\_helper"}\OperatorTok{ \}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsection{19.3 Managing Dependencies}\label{managing-dependencies-1}

Add dependencies easily using the CLI:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\ExtensionTok{iris}\NormalTok{ pkg add http\_utils }\AttributeTok{{-}{-}git}\NormalTok{ https://github.com/iris{-}lang/http\_utils.git}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

This automatically downloads, validates, and adds the dependency to your
\texttt{iris.toml}.

\subsection{19.4 Package Subcommands}\label{package-subcommands-1}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{iris\ pkg\ build}}: Resolves dependencies, compiles
  them, and builds the current package.
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{iris\ pkg\ run}}: Compiles and runs the package entry
  point.
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{iris\ pkg\ update}}: Updates lockfile and checks for
  newer compatible dependency versions.
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{iris\ pkg\ list}}: Lists all active project
  dependencies.
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{iris\ pkg\ check}}: Rapidly parses and checks package
  types without full compilation.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Try It Yourself}\label{try-it-yourself-18}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Run \texttt{iris\ pkg\ init\ calc\_project} to initialize a new
  package.
\item
  Edit \texttt{iris.toml} to set yourself as the author.
\item
  Build and run it using \texttt{iris\ pkg\ run}.
\end{enumerate}

\section{Chapter 20: Working with
Databases}\label{chapter-20-working-with-databases}

IRIS includes built-in support for \textbf{SQLite} databases. You can
create, query, and manage local databases without importing any
libraries --- the four database builtins are part of the language.

\subsection{20.1 The Database API}\label{the-database-api}

\begin{longtable}[]{@{}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 4\tabcolsep) * \real{0.2857}}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 4\tabcolsep) * \real{0.3214}}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 4\tabcolsep) * \real{0.3929}}@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
\begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Function
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Signature
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Description
\end{minipage} \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
\texttt{db\_open} & \texttt{db\_open(path:\ str)\ -\textgreater{}\ i64}
& Open (or create) a SQLite database file. Returns a handle. \\
\texttt{db\_exec} &
\texttt{db\_exec(db:\ i64,\ sql:\ str)\ -\textgreater{}\ i64} & Execute
a statement (CREATE, INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE). Returns 0 on success, -1
on error. \\
\texttt{db\_query} &
\texttt{db\_query(db:\ i64,\ sql:\ str)\ -\textgreater{}\ list\textless{}list\textless{}str\textgreater{}\textgreater{}}
& Execute a SELECT query. Returns a list of rows, each row a list of
string columns. \\
\texttt{db\_close} & \texttt{db\_close(db:\ i64)\ -\textgreater{}\ i64}
& Close the database handle. Returns 0. \\
\end{longtable}

All values returned by \texttt{db\_query} are strings --- you convert to
numbers with \texttt{to\_i64()} or \texttt{to\_f64()} as needed.

\subsection{20.2 Creating a Database and
Table}\label{creating-a-database-and-table}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ main() }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ db }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ db\_open(}\StringTok{"app.db"}\NormalTok{);}
\NormalTok{    db\_exec(db, }\StringTok{"CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS users (id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT, name TEXT, age INTEGER)"}\NormalTok{);}
    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"Table created"}\NormalTok{);}
\NormalTok{    db\_close(db)}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

If the file \texttt{app.db} does not exist, \texttt{db\_open} creates it
automatically. The handle is an opaque integer --- pass it to every
subsequent database call.

\subsection{20.3 Inserting Data}\label{inserting-data}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ main() }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ db }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ db\_open(}\StringTok{"app.db"}\NormalTok{);}
\NormalTok{    db\_exec(db, }\StringTok{"CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS users (id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT, name TEXT, age INTEGER)"}\NormalTok{);}
\NormalTok{    db\_exec(db, }\StringTok{"INSERT INTO users (name, age) VALUES (\textquotesingle{}Alice\textquotesingle{}, 30)"}\NormalTok{);}
\NormalTok{    db\_exec(db, }\StringTok{"INSERT INTO users (name, age) VALUES (\textquotesingle{}Bob\textquotesingle{}, 25)"}\NormalTok{);}
\NormalTok{    db\_exec(db, }\StringTok{"INSERT INTO users (name, age) VALUES (\textquotesingle{}Carol\textquotesingle{}, 28)"}\NormalTok{);}
    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"Inserted 3 users"}\NormalTok{);}
\NormalTok{    db\_close(db)}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Each \texttt{db\_exec} call runs a single SQL statement. Check the
return value: 0 means success, -1 means the statement failed.

\subsection{20.4 Querying Data}\label{querying-data}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ main() }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ db }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ db\_open(}\StringTok{"app.db"}\NormalTok{);}
\NormalTok{    db\_exec(db, }\StringTok{"CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS users (id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT, name TEXT, age INTEGER)"}\NormalTok{);}
\NormalTok{    db\_exec(db, }\StringTok{"INSERT INTO users (name, age) VALUES (\textquotesingle{}Alice\textquotesingle{}, 30)"}\NormalTok{);}
\NormalTok{    db\_exec(db, }\StringTok{"INSERT INTO users (name, age) VALUES (\textquotesingle{}Bob\textquotesingle{}, 25)"}\NormalTok{);}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ rows }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ db\_query(db, }\StringTok{"SELECT name, age FROM users"}\NormalTok{);}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ n }\OperatorTok{=} \FunctionTok{list\_len}\NormalTok{(rows);}
    \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ idx }\ControlFlowTok{in} \DecValTok{0}\OperatorTok{..}\NormalTok{n \{}
        \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ row }\OperatorTok{=} \FunctionTok{list\_get}\NormalTok{(rows, idx);}
        \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ name }\OperatorTok{=} \FunctionTok{list\_get}\NormalTok{(row, }\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{);}
        \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ age }\OperatorTok{=} \FunctionTok{list\_get}\NormalTok{(row, }\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{);}
        \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(name);}
        \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(age)}
\NormalTok{    \}}
\NormalTok{    db\_close(db)}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\texttt{db\_query} returns a
\texttt{list\textless{}list\textless{}str\textgreater{}\textgreater{}}.
Each inner list is one row. Column values are always strings --- use
\texttt{to\_i64()} or \texttt{to\_f64()} if you need numeric types.

\subsection{20.5 Parameterized Queries}\label{parameterized-queries}

Use \texttt{db\_exec\_params} and \texttt{db\_query\_params} when values
should be bound separately from the SQL text.

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ main() }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ db }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ db\_open(}\StringTok{"app.db"}\NormalTok{);}
\NormalTok{    db\_exec(db, }\StringTok{"CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS users (id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT, name TEXT, age INTEGER)"}\NormalTok{);}

    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ insert\_params }\OperatorTok{=} \DataTypeTok{list}\NormalTok{();}
\NormalTok{    list\_push(insert\_params, }\StringTok{"Alice"}\NormalTok{);}
\NormalTok{    list\_push(insert\_params, }\StringTok{"30"}\NormalTok{);}
\NormalTok{    db\_exec\_params(db, }\StringTok{"INSERT INTO users (name, age) VALUES (?, ?)"}\NormalTok{, insert\_params);}

    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ lookup\_params }\OperatorTok{=} \DataTypeTok{list}\NormalTok{();}
\NormalTok{    list\_push(lookup\_params, }\StringTok{"Alice"}\NormalTok{);}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ rows }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ db\_query\_params(db, }\StringTok{"SELECT name, age FROM users WHERE name = ?"}\NormalTok{, lookup\_params);}

    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{concat}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"matched rows = "}\NormalTok{, }\FunctionTok{to\_str}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{list\_len}\NormalTok{(rows))));}
\NormalTok{    db\_close(db)}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

The \texttt{std.sql} module also exposes \texttt{sql\_exec\_params},
\texttt{sql\_query\_params}, and \texttt{sql\_query\_xy\_params} as thin
wrappers over the same builtins.

\subsection{20.6 Updating and Deleting}\label{updating-and-deleting}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ main() }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ db }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ db\_open(}\StringTok{"app.db"}\NormalTok{);}
\NormalTok{    db\_exec(db, }\StringTok{"CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS users (id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT, name TEXT, age INTEGER)"}\NormalTok{);}
\NormalTok{    db\_exec(db, }\StringTok{"INSERT INTO users (name, age) VALUES (\textquotesingle{}Alice\textquotesingle{}, 30)"}\NormalTok{);}
\NormalTok{    db\_exec(db, }\StringTok{"UPDATE users SET age = 31 WHERE name = \textquotesingle{}Alice\textquotesingle{}"}\NormalTok{);}
\NormalTok{    db\_exec(db, }\StringTok{"DELETE FROM users WHERE name = \textquotesingle{}Alice\textquotesingle{}"}\NormalTok{);}
\NormalTok{    db\_close(db)}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

UPDATE and DELETE are executed with \texttt{db\_exec} just like INSERT
and CREATE.

\subsection{20.7 Error Handling}\label{error-handling}

Always check the return value of \texttt{db\_exec}:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ main() }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ db }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ db\_open(}\StringTok{"app.db"}\NormalTok{);}
    \KeywordTok{val} \DataTypeTok{result} \OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ db\_exec(db, }\StringTok{"THIS IS NOT VALID SQL"}\NormalTok{);}
    \ControlFlowTok{if} \DataTypeTok{result} \OperatorTok{==} \OperatorTok{{-}}\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{ \{}
        \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"SQL error"}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    \};}
\NormalTok{    db\_close(db)}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

If \texttt{db\_open} fails (e.g.~invalid path), it returns 0. Always
verify the handle before using it.

\subsection{20.8 A Complete Example: Task
Manager}\label{a-complete-example-task-manager}

Here is a small task-management database that creates a table, inserts
tasks, marks one complete, and queries the results:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ main() }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ db }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ db\_open(}\StringTok{"tasks.db"}\NormalTok{);}
\NormalTok{    db\_exec(db, }\StringTok{"CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS tasks (id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT, title TEXT, done INTEGER DEFAULT 0)"}\NormalTok{);}
\NormalTok{    db\_exec(db, }\StringTok{"DELETE FROM tasks"}\NormalTok{);}
\NormalTok{    db\_exec(db, }\StringTok{"INSERT INTO tasks (title) VALUES (\textquotesingle{}Write docs\textquotesingle{})"}\NormalTok{);}
\NormalTok{    db\_exec(db, }\StringTok{"INSERT INTO tasks (title) VALUES (\textquotesingle{}Fix bug\textquotesingle{})"}\NormalTok{);}
\NormalTok{    db\_exec(db, }\StringTok{"INSERT INTO tasks (title) VALUES (\textquotesingle{}Add tests\textquotesingle{})"}\NormalTok{);}
\NormalTok{    db\_exec(db, }\StringTok{"UPDATE tasks SET done = 1 WHERE title = \textquotesingle{}Fix bug\textquotesingle{}"}\NormalTok{);}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ rows }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ db\_query(db, }\StringTok{"SELECT title, done FROM tasks ORDER BY id"}\NormalTok{);}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ n }\OperatorTok{=} \FunctionTok{list\_len}\NormalTok{(rows);}
    \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ idx }\ControlFlowTok{in} \DecValTok{0}\OperatorTok{..}\NormalTok{n \{}
        \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ row }\OperatorTok{=} \FunctionTok{list\_get}\NormalTok{(rows, idx);}
        \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ title }\OperatorTok{=} \FunctionTok{list\_get}\NormalTok{(row, }\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{);}
        \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ done }\OperatorTok{=} \FunctionTok{list\_get}\NormalTok{(row, }\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{);}
        \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(title);}
        \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(done)}
\NormalTok{    \}}
\NormalTok{    db\_close(db)}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsection{20.8 Best Practices}\label{best-practices}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Always close} the database handle with \texttt{db\_close} when
  you are finished.
\item
  \textbf{Use \texttt{IF\ NOT\ EXISTS}} on CREATE TABLE so your program
  can run more than once.
\item
  \textbf{Check return codes} from \texttt{db\_exec} --- a return of -1
  indicates an error.
\item
  \textbf{Delete or clean up} test databases after use
  (\texttt{db\_exec(db,\ "DROP\ TABLE\ ...")} or delete the file).
\item
  \textbf{All query values are strings} --- convert with
  \texttt{to\_i64()} or \texttt{to\_f64()} when needed.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{20.11 UDP Networking}\label{udp-networking}

For low-latency communication, IRIS features high-performance UDP socket
support:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ udp\_demo() }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \DataTypeTok{i64}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \CommentTok{// Open a UDP socket bound to local port 8080}
    \KeywordTok{val}\NormalTok{ socket }\OperatorTok{=} \FunctionTok{udp\_open}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"127.0.0.1:8080"}\NormalTok{);}
    
    \CommentTok{// Send a datagram}
    \FunctionTok{udp\_send}\NormalTok{(socket, }\StringTok{"127.0.0.1:8081"}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{"Ping"}\NormalTok{);}
    
    \CommentTok{// Receive a datagram (blocks until received)}
    \KeywordTok{val} \DataTypeTok{result} \OperatorTok{=} \FunctionTok{udp\_recv}\NormalTok{(socket);}
    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{concat}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"From: "}\NormalTok{, }\DataTypeTok{result}\OperatorTok{.}\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{)); }\CommentTok{// Sender address}
    \FunctionTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{concat}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"Data: "}\NormalTok{, }\DataTypeTok{result}\OperatorTok{.}\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{)); }\CommentTok{// Payload}
    
    \FunctionTok{udp\_close}\NormalTok{(socket);}
    \DecValTok{0}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsection{Try It Yourself}\label{try-it-yourself-19}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Build a contacts database that stores name, email, and phone number.
  Write functions to add, search, and delete contacts.
\item
  Create a simple inventory system: insert items with name, quantity,
  and price, then query for items below a certain stock level.
\item
  Write a program that imports data from a file (using
  \texttt{file\_read\_all}) and inserts each line as a row in a database
  table.
\end{enumerate}

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\section{Chapter 21: Security \&
Sandboxing}\label{chapter-21-security-sandboxing}

IRIS provides enterprise-grade runtime sandboxing capabilities to
execute untrusted code safely.

\subsection{21.1 The Sandbox Flag}\label{the-sandbox-flag}

By running the compiler with the \texttt{-\/-sandbox} flag, the IRIS C
runtime restricts access to operating system capabilities:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\ExtensionTok{iris}\NormalTok{ run }\AttributeTok{{-}{-}sandbox}\NormalTok{ untrusted\_script.iris}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsection{21.2 Restricted Operations}\label{restricted-operations}

When running in sandbox mode, the following operations are strictly
blocked and cause an immediate runtime panic:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Filesystem}: File read/write operations outside designated
  whitelist directories are rejected.
\item
  \textbf{Networking}: Unauthorized outbound TCP/UDP connections or
  inbound listening sockets are denied.
\item
  \textbf{Processes}: System command execution (\texttt{exec\_cmd},
  \texttt{pid}) is blocked.
\item
  \textbf{FFI}: Foreign Function Interface modules (\texttt{std.ffi},
  \texttt{ffi\_open}) are disabled to prevent bypassing sandbox rules.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{21.3 Customizing Whitelists}\label{customizing-whitelists}

You can grant selective access to resources using sandbox flags:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\ExtensionTok{iris}\NormalTok{ run }\AttributeTok{{-}{-}sandbox} \AttributeTok{{-}{-}allow{-}read}\NormalTok{ ./data/ }\AttributeTok{{-}{-}allow{-}net}\NormalTok{ api.example.com script.iris}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsection{Try It Yourself}\label{try-it-yourself-20}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Write a script \texttt{test\_sec.iris} that attempts to read
  \texttt{/etc/passwd} or
  \texttt{C:\textbackslash{}\textbackslash{}Windows\textbackslash{}\textbackslash{}system.ini}.
\item
  Run it without flags: \texttt{iris\ run\ test\_sec.iris}.
\item
  Run it with the sandbox flag:
  \texttt{iris\ run\ -\/-sandbox\ test\_sec.iris} and observe the
  sandbox denial panic.
\end{enumerate}

\section{Appendix A: Language Grammar
(BNF)}\label{appendix-a-language-grammar-bnf}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{module      ::= \{ top\_level \}}
\NormalTok{top\_level   ::= function\_def}
\NormalTok{              | record\_def}
\NormalTok{              | enum\_def}
\NormalTok{              | const\_def}
\NormalTok{              | type\_alias}
\NormalTok{              | trait\_def}
\NormalTok{              | impl\_def}
\NormalTok{              | bring\_decl}
\NormalTok{              | extern\_def}
\NormalTok{              | model\_def}

\NormalTok{bring\_decl  ::= "bring" bring\_path}
\NormalTok{bring\_path  ::= IDENT \{ "." IDENT \}}
\NormalTok{              | STRING\_LIT}

\NormalTok{function\_def ::= [ "pub" ] [ "async" ] "def" IDENT [ type\_params ] "(" params ")" "{-}\textgreater{}" type block}
\NormalTok{type\_params  ::= "[" IDENT \{ "," IDENT \} "]"}
\NormalTok{params       ::= [ param \{ "," param \} ]}
\NormalTok{param        ::= IDENT ":" type [ "=" expr ]}

\NormalTok{record\_def  ::= [ "pub" ] "record" IDENT "\{" field\_defs "\}"}
\NormalTok{field\_defs  ::= field\_def \{ "," field\_def \}}
\NormalTok{field\_def   ::= IDENT ":" type}

\NormalTok{enum\_def    ::= [ "pub" ] "choice" IDENT "\{" variant\_defs "\}"}
\NormalTok{variant\_defs ::= variant\_def \{ "," variant\_def \}}
\NormalTok{variant\_def  ::= IDENT [ "(" type \{ "," type \} ")" ]}

\NormalTok{const\_def   ::= [ "pub" ] "const" IDENT [ ":" type ] "=" expr}

\NormalTok{type\_alias  ::= [ "pub" ] "type" IDENT "=" type}

\NormalTok{trait\_def   ::= "trait" IDENT "\{" \{ trait\_method \} "\}"}
\NormalTok{trait\_method ::= "def" IDENT "(" params ")" "{-}\textgreater{}" type}

\NormalTok{impl\_def    ::= "impl" IDENT "for" IDENT "\{" \{ function\_def \} "\}"}

\NormalTok{extern\_def  ::= "extern" "def" IDENT "(" params ")" "{-}\textgreater{}" type}

\NormalTok{model\_def   ::= "model" IDENT "\{" \{ model\_item \} "\}"}
\NormalTok{model\_item  ::= "input" IDENT ":" type}
\NormalTok{              | "layer" IDENT IDENT [ "(" layer\_args ")" ]}
\NormalTok{              | "output" IDENT}
\NormalTok{layer\_args  ::= layer\_arg \{ "," layer\_arg \}}
\NormalTok{layer\_arg   ::= IDENT "=" expr | IDENT}

\NormalTok{(* Statements *)}
\NormalTok{block       ::= "\{" \{ stmt \} [ expr ] "\}"}
\NormalTok{stmt        ::= let\_stmt}
\NormalTok{              | assign\_stmt}
\NormalTok{              | while\_stmt}
\NormalTok{              | loop\_stmt}
\NormalTok{              | for\_stmt}
\NormalTok{              | par\_for\_stmt}
\NormalTok{              | spawn\_stmt}
\NormalTok{              | return\_stmt}
\NormalTok{              | break\_stmt}
\NormalTok{              | continue\_stmt}
\NormalTok{              | expr ";"}

\NormalTok{let\_stmt    ::= "val" IDENT [ ":" type ] "=" expr ";"}
\NormalTok{              | "var" IDENT [ ":" type ] "=" expr ";"}
\NormalTok{              | "val" "(" IDENT \{ "," IDENT \} ")" "=" expr ";"}
\NormalTok{assign\_stmt ::= expr "=" expr ";"}
\NormalTok{while\_stmt  ::= "while" expr block}
\NormalTok{loop\_stmt   ::= "loop" block}
\NormalTok{for\_stmt    ::= "for" IDENT "in" expr ".." expr block}
\NormalTok{              | "for" IDENT "in" expr block}
\NormalTok{par\_for\_stmt ::= "par" "for" IDENT "in" expr ".." expr block}
\NormalTok{spawn\_stmt  ::= "spawn" block}
\NormalTok{return\_stmt ::= "return" [ expr ] ";"}
\NormalTok{break\_stmt  ::= "break" ";"}
\NormalTok{continue\_stmt ::= "continue" ";"}

\NormalTok{(* Expressions — from lowest to highest precedence *)}
\NormalTok{expr        ::= or\_expr}
\NormalTok{or\_expr     ::= and\_expr \{ "||" and\_expr \}}
\NormalTok{and\_expr    ::= cmp\_expr \{ "\&\&" cmp\_expr \}}
\NormalTok{cmp\_expr    ::= add\_expr \{ ( "==" | "!=" | "\textless{}" | "\textless{}=" | "\textgreater{}" | "\textgreater{}=" ) add\_expr \}}
\NormalTok{add\_expr    ::= mul\_expr \{ ( "+" | "{-}" ) mul\_expr \}}
\NormalTok{mul\_expr    ::= cast\_expr \{ ( "*" | "/" | "\%" ) cast\_expr \}}
\NormalTok{cast\_expr   ::= unary\_expr [ "to" type ]}
\NormalTok{unary\_expr  ::= [ "{-}" | "!" ] postfix\_expr}
\NormalTok{postfix\_expr ::= primary \{ "." IDENT [ "(" args ")" ] | "." INT\_LIT | "[" args "]" | "?" \}}

\NormalTok{primary     ::= INT\_LIT}
\NormalTok{              | FLOAT\_LIT}
\NormalTok{              | BOOL\_LIT}
\NormalTok{              | STRING\_LIT}
\NormalTok{              | FSTRING\_LIT}
\NormalTok{              | IDENT [ "::" IDENT ] [ "(" args ")" ]}
\NormalTok{              | IDENT "\{" field\_inits "\}"}
\NormalTok{              | "(" expr \{ "," expr \} ")"}
\NormalTok{              | "[" [ expr \{ "," expr \} ] "]"}
\NormalTok{              | "|" params "|" expr}
\NormalTok{              | "if" expr block [ "else" block ]}
\NormalTok{              | "when" expr "\{" when\_arms "\}"}
\NormalTok{              | "await" expr}
\NormalTok{              | block}

\NormalTok{args        ::= [ expr \{ "," expr \} ]}
\NormalTok{field\_inits ::= [ IDENT ":" expr \{ "," IDENT ":" expr \} ]}

\NormalTok{when\_arms   ::= when\_arm \{ "," when\_arm \}}
\NormalTok{when\_arm    ::= pattern [ "if" expr ] "=\textgreater{}" expr}
\NormalTok{pattern     ::= IDENT "." IDENT [ "(" bindings ")" ]}
\NormalTok{              | "some" "(" IDENT ")"}
\NormalTok{              | "none"}
\NormalTok{              | "ok" "(" IDENT ")"}
\NormalTok{              | "err" "(" IDENT ")"}
\NormalTok{              | INT\_LIT [ "..=" INT\_LIT ]}
\NormalTok{              | BOOL\_LIT}
\NormalTok{              | STRING\_LIT}
\NormalTok{              | "(" pattern \{ "," pattern \} ")"}
\NormalTok{              | "\_"}
\NormalTok{bindings    ::= [ IDENT \{ "," IDENT \} ]}

\NormalTok{(* Types *)}
\NormalTok{type        ::= scalar\_type}
\NormalTok{              | "tensor" "\textless{}" scalar\_type "," "[" dims "]" "\textgreater{}"}
\NormalTok{              | "option" "\textless{}" type "\textgreater{}"}
\NormalTok{              | "result" "\textless{}" type "," type "\textgreater{}"}
\NormalTok{              | "channel" "\textless{}" type "\textgreater{}"}
\NormalTok{              | "atomic" "\textless{}" type "\textgreater{}"}
\NormalTok{              | "mutex" "\textless{}" type "\textgreater{}"}
\NormalTok{              | "grad" "\textless{}" type "\textgreater{}"}
\NormalTok{              | "sparse" "\textless{}" type "\textgreater{}"}
\NormalTok{              | "list" "\textless{}" type "\textgreater{}"}
\NormalTok{              | "map" "\textless{}" type "," type "\textgreater{}"}
\NormalTok{              | "[" type ";" INT\_LIT "]"}
\NormalTok{              | "(" type \{ "," type \} ")"}
\NormalTok{              | "(" [ type \{ "," type \} ] ")" "{-}\textgreater{}" type}
\NormalTok{              | IDENT  (* named struct/enum/alias *)}

\NormalTok{scalar\_type ::= "i8" | "u8" | "i32" | "u32" | "i64" | "u64" | "usize"}
\NormalTok{              | "f32" | "f64" | "bool" | "str"}

\NormalTok{dims        ::= dim \{ "," dim \}}
\NormalTok{dim         ::= INT\_LIT | IDENT}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\section{Appendix B: Built-in Functions
Reference}\label{appendix-b-built-in-functions-reference}

\texttt{log2}, \texttt{sqrt}, \texttt{abs}, \texttt{floor},

\texttt{math\_e}, \texttt{math\_inf}, \texttt{is\_nan}, \texttt{is\_inf}

\texttt{ends\_with}, \texttt{to\_upper}, \texttt{to\_lower},
\texttt{trim}, \texttt{repeat}, \texttt{to\_str}, \texttt{format},
\texttt{split}, \texttt{join}, \texttt{find}, \texttt{slice},
\texttt{str\_index}, \texttt{str\_replace}, \texttt{str\_reverse},
\texttt{char\_at}, \texttt{str\_pad\_left}, \texttt{str\_pad\_right},
\texttt{str\_chars}, \texttt{str\_bytes}, \texttt{str\_count}

\texttt{band(a,\ b)}, \texttt{bor(a,\ b)}, \texttt{bxor(a,\ b)},
\texttt{shl(a,\ n)}, \texttt{shr(a,\ n)}, \texttt{bitnot(a)}

\texttt{print}, \texttt{read\_line}, \texttt{read\_i64},
\texttt{read\_f64}

\begin{itemize}

  \texttt{list\_pop}, \texttt{list\_map}, \texttt{list\_filter},

\section{Appendix C: Type System
Reference}\label{appendix-c-type-system-reference}

\subsection{Scalar Types}\label{scalar-types}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Integers}: \texttt{i8} (8-bit signed), \texttt{u8} (8-bit
  unsigned), \texttt{i32} (32-bit signed), \texttt{u32} (32-bit
  unsigned), \texttt{i64} (64-bit signed), \texttt{u64} (64-bit
  unsigned), \texttt{usize} (pointer-sized unsigned)
\item
  \textbf{Floats}: \texttt{f32} (32-bit single precision), \texttt{f64}
  (64-bit double precision)
\item
  \textbf{Booleans}: \texttt{bool} (\texttt{true}, \texttt{false})
\item
  \textbf{Strings}: \texttt{str} (UTF-8 immutable sequence)
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Composite Types}\label{composite-types}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Tensors}:
  \texttt{tensor\textless{}scalar\_type,\ {[}dimensions{]}\textgreater{}}
\item
  \textbf{Lists}: \texttt{list\textless{}T\textgreater{}}
\item
  \textbf{Maps}: \texttt{map\textless{}K,\ V\textgreater{}}
\item
  \textbf{Deques}: \texttt{deque}
\item
  \textbf{BitSets}: \texttt{bitset}
\item
  \textbf{Mutexes}: \texttt{mutex\textless{}T\textgreater{}}
\item
  \textbf{Channels}: \texttt{channel\textless{}T\textgreater{}}
\item
  \textbf{Reference Cells}: \texttt{cell\textless{}T\textgreater{}}
\item
  \textbf{Automatic Differentiation}:
  \texttt{grad\textless{}T\textgreater{}}
\item
  \textbf{Sparse Tensors}: \texttt{sparse\textless{}T\textgreater{}}
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Operator Precedence (highest to
lowest)}\label{operator-precedence-highest-to-lowest}

\begin{longtable}[]{@{}llll@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
Precedence & Category & Operators & Associativity \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
1 (highest) & Postfix & \texttt{.field} \texttt{.method()}
\texttt{{[}index{]}} \texttt{?} & Left \\
2 & Prefix & \texttt{-} (negate) \texttt{!} (not) & Right \\
3 & Multiplicative & \texttt{*} \texttt{/} \texttt{\%} & Left \\
4 & Additive & \texttt{+} \texttt{-} & Left \\
5 & Cast & \texttt{to} & Left \\
6 & Comparison & \texttt{==} \texttt{!=} \texttt{\textless{}}
\texttt{\textless{}=} \texttt{\textgreater{}} \texttt{\textgreater{}=} &
Left, non-chaining \\
7 & Logical AND & \texttt{\&\&} & Left, short-circuit \\
8 (lowest) & Logical OR & \texttt{\textbar{}\textbar{}} & Left,
short-circuit \\
\end{longtable}

\section{Appendix D: CLI Reference}\label{appendix-d-cli-reference}

The \texttt{iris} compiler provides a single, unified CLI with 10
powerful subcommands:

\subsection{10 Subcommands}\label{subcommands}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{build\ \textless{}file.iris\textgreater{}}}: Compiles
  an IRIS source file into a native binary.
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{run\ \textless{}file.iris\textgreater{}}}: Compiles
  and executes an IRIS program directly.
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{repl}}: Starts the interactive REPL shell.
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{lsp}}: Launches the background LSP Language Server.
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{dap}}: Launches the Debug Adapter Protocol server.
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{pkg}}: Package manager operations (init, build, run,
  add, update, list, check).
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{bench\ \textless{}file.iris\textgreater{}}}: Runs
  benchmarks tagged with \texttt{@bench}.
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{profile\ \textless{}file.iris\textgreater{}}}: Runs
  the compiler profiler and outputs execution flame graphs.
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{test}}: Discovers and runs test cases in the
  workspace.
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{explain\ \textless{}code\textgreater{}}}: Interactive
  diagnostic code explanation catalog.
\end{enumerate}

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{14 Emit Kinds
(\texttt{-\/-emit\ \textless{}kind\textgreater{}})}{14 Emit Kinds (-\/-emit \textless kind\textgreater)}}\label{emit-kinds---emit-kind}

Specify intermediate compiler outputs: - \textbf{\texttt{eval}}: Direct
evaluation in the AST interpreter. - \textbf{\texttt{tokens}}: Prints
lexical tokens. - \textbf{\texttt{ast}}: Prints structural Abstract
Syntax Tree. - \textbf{\texttt{ir}}: Prints text SSA Intermediate
Representation. - \textbf{\texttt{ir-opt}}: SSA IR after optimization
passes. - \textbf{\texttt{llvm}}: Text LLVM Assembly. -
\textbf{\texttt{bc}}: LLVM Bitcode file. - \textbf{\texttt{asm}}: Target
assembly code. - \textbf{\texttt{obj}}: Compiled object file. -
\textbf{\texttt{binary}}: Native executable file. -
\textbf{\texttt{onnx}}: Exported ONNX model graph. -
\textbf{\texttt{cuda}}: Generated CUDA source code. -
\textbf{\texttt{simd}}: Vectorized IR output. - \textbf{\texttt{graph}}:
Generates AST or IR visual dependency dot files.

\subsection{Global Flags}\label{global-flags}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{-\/-sandbox}}: Strict runtime sandboxing.
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{-\/-target\ \textless{}triple\textgreater{}}}:
  Cross-compilation target.
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{-\/-no-cache}}: Disables AST and LLVM caching.
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{-\/-dump-ir-after\ \textless{}pass\textgreater{}}}:
  Dumps compiler state after specific optimizer pass.
\end{itemize}

\section{Appendix E: Compiler Error
Reference}\label{appendix-e-compiler-error-reference}

IRIS has a detailed diagnostic code system cross-referenced directly
with the \texttt{iris\ explain} command.

\subsection{Diagnostic Code Catalog}\label{diagnostic-code-catalog}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{E1:\ Missing\ else\ branch}}: Every \texttt{if}
  expression must have a matching \texttt{else} block to guarantee a
  returned value.
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{E2:\ Missing\ semicolon\ after\ non-tail\ statement}}:
  Semicolons are required to separate non-tail statements in blocks.
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{E3:\ Reassigning\ an\ immutable\ binding}}: Attempting
  to reassign a \texttt{val} binding instead of a \texttt{var} binding.
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{E4:\ Type\ mismatch\ in\ binary\ operation}}:
  Operators require both operands to have the same type. IRIS does not
  perform implicit type casting.
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{E5:\ Float\ literal\ type}}: Floating-point literal
  mismatch. Remember that float literals are \texttt{f64} by default.
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{E6:\ Calling\ unwrap\ on\ none}}: Unsafely calling
  \texttt{unwrap} on an option that contains \texttt{none}. Always check
  with \texttt{is\_some()}.
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{E7:\ Operator\ precedence\ with\ comparison}}: Parsing
  error because operators like \texttt{+} have different precedence
  relative to comparisons.
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{E8:\ find\ result\ used\ as\ number}}: Attempting to
  use the \texttt{option\textless{}i64\textgreater{}} returned by
  \texttt{find} directly in arithmetic.
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{E9:\ Function\ not\ exported}}: Calling a function
  from another module that has not been marked with \texttt{pub}.
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{E10:\ Using\ \%\ modulo\ vs\ /\ division}}: Diagnostic
  error checking division operators.
\end{itemize}
